# Level C1: Effective operational proficiency

**Prerequisite:** Milestone B2 reached (the last step of 04-B2.md ticked). The README methods still apply; the day continues in the C1 form below.

**Goal at exit:** you can follow lectures, debates, satire and fast group conversation without effort, including the France Inter morning, *Les Grosses Têtes* and a Québec talk show; read literary prose of any period from Montaigne in modernised spelling to this year's *rentrée littéraire*, and academic and journalistic French with full understanding of tone, irony and register; speak fluently and precisely on abstract and specialised topics, adjusting register at will from *soutenu* to *familier*; and write long, well-structured texts (a *commentaire composé* and a *dissertation* in the EAF form, a bac philosophy *dissertation*, a DALF *synthèse*, a 2000-word *essai*) with only occasional slips, in a spelling that passes the Certificat Voltaire. Vocabulary about 12,000 words. You recognise the French of Québec, Belgium, Switzerland, the Maghreb, West Africa and the Antilles by ear, can read a 1694 dictionary page and an 1857 *Madame Bovary* in Gallica, and can produce the *passé simple* and the *subjonctif imparfait* in writing. This is the level of the DALF C1 and of the reading a French *classe préparatoire* or university expects.

**Time:** the dailies take about 2 hours 25 minutes a day (the table's 140 minutes plus the Sunday correction batch of the *journal de lecture*, spread over the week; half of it is the commute slot and free reading, which need no desk) and the steps add up to about 585 hours: about 1100 hours in all. At the planned pace, over 36 weeks, that is 2 hours 45 minutes of steps a day, 6 days a week; at one hour of steps a day the level takes about 98 weeks. Keep the order either way.

**What changes at C1** (Appendix A): the level is organised around literature and public intellectual life. Twenty-eight works from the canon and the present, each read as a French *lycéen* who sat the EAF would read it: with *Lagarde et Michard*, a scholarly introduction and a *commentaire composé* or an essay afterwards. Grammar is finished as a course; what remains is the written language in full (the *passé simple* and the *subjonctif imparfait* produced, the *concordance des temps*, the agreements in every case, the 1990 rectifications beside the traditional spelling, the typography) with Grevisse as the authority, and the lexicon by register and domain. Listening moves to France Culture, Collège de France lectures, satire and the Francophonie: one block for Québec, one for Belgium, Switzerland, Africa and the Antilles. Writing reaches the EAF *commentaire*, the bac philosophy *dissertation* and the DALF *synthèse*. Underneath, the dailies continue: the commute slot carries the audiobooks, the journal becomes a *journal de lecture*, the daily song becomes the daily poem or song, and a five-minute shadowing of a France Inter presenter is added. Every week has a *Le Bureau des légendes* episode (seasons 2 to 5), a second series, a *Canard enchaîné* step, two or three radio steps, a *Lagarde et Michard* chapter, a dictée or Projet Voltaire session, a 12-minute exposé or an AI debate; every two weeks a *commentaire* or *dissertation*; every three weeks a *Que sais-je ?*. Films go from sixteen with subtitles ending to sixteen without any.

**Resources used at C1** (acquisition notes in Appendix L):

| Code | Resource | Role |
|---|---|---|
| BU | Maurice Grevisse and André Goosse, *Le Bon Usage* (De Boeck Supérieur, 16th edition or the current one) | The authority; selected chapters, Weeks 1–33 |
| GPP | *Grammaire progressive du français, niveau perfectionnement* (CLE International, with the *corrigés*) | The exercise book beside BU, Weeks 1–18 |
| DICT | *Le Petit Robert* (the current edition, paper or app); the TLFi at cnrtl.fr; the Académie française's *Dire, ne pas dire* column; the Office québécois's *Banque de dépannage linguistique* (bdl.oqlf.gouv.qc.ca); Antidote | Dictionaries and usage, all level |
| PV | *Projet Voltaire* (projet-voltaire.fr) to the Certificat Voltaire | Spelling, one session every two weeks; the certificate or its full practice test at the exit |
| EAF | A *Français 1re* textbook (Nathan, Hatier or Magnard, any recent edition); the EAF past papers and *grilles* on eduscol.education.fr and the académie sites | *Commentaire composé*, *dissertation*, figures, versification, Weeks 1–14 |
| PHILO | A *Terminale* philosophy manual (Hatier, Nathan, Bordas or Magnard; second-hand) and the bac *annales* with *corrigés* | The philosophy dissertation, Weeks 28–29 |

| QSJ | *Que sais-je ?* (PUF; 128-page volumes by specialists), twelve over the level, each paired with a France Culture programme | Specialised domains, one every three weeks |
| PROUST | Marcel Proust, *Du côté de chez Swann* (Folio classique or GF), with the audiobook | The C1 mountain, Weeks 1–6 |
| XX | Camus *La Chute*; Queneau *Exercices de style*; Sartre *Les Mots*; Ernaux *Les Années*; Queneau *Zazie dans le métro*; Perec *Les Choses* | Block 1 and the ends of Blocks 2 and 4 |
| XIX | Flaubert *Madame Bovary*; Balzac *Le Père Goriot*; Nerval *Sylvie*; Stendhal *Le Rouge et le Noir* (+ audiobook); Zola *Germinal* (+ audiobook, the 1993 film) | Blocks 2 and 3 |
| XVII | Montaigne, three *Essais* in modernised spelling; Pascal, *Pensées* (a selection of 40); Mme de La Fayette *La Princesse de Clèves*; Racine *Phèdre*; Molière *Tartuffe* and *Le Misanthrope* (with Comédie-Française recordings) | Block 3 |
| XVIII | Beaumarchais *Le Mariage de Figaro*; Laclos *Les Liaisons dangereuses* (with the 1988 film); Rousseau *Confessions* I–II; Diderot *Jacques le fataliste* as free reading | Block 4 |
| FRANCO-LIT | Michel Tremblay *Les Belles-sœurs* (Québec); Aimé Césaire *Cahier d'un retour au pays natal*; Kamel Daoud *Meursault, contre-enquête*; Alain Mabanckou *Verre cassé* | The Francophonie blocks |
| CONTEMP | Marguerite Yourcenar *Mémoires d'Hadrien*; Pierre Lemaitre *Au revoir là-haut* (+ audiobook, the 2017 film); Marie NDiaye *Trois femmes puissantes* | Weeks 26–32 |
| TV-A | *Le Bureau des légendes* seasons 2–5 (Canal+; abroad on the platform that carries it, see Appendix L) | Television spine, one episode a week |
| TV-B | *En thérapie* S1 (Arte), *Hippocrate* S1 (Canal+), *La Trêve* S1 (RTBF; Netflix), *Les Sauvages* (Canal+), *Engrenages* S2 (Canal+), *Baron noir* S1 (Canal+) | Second and third series, one step a week each |
| TV-C | *Quotidien* (TMC, free on tf1.fr), *28 minutes* and *C ce soir* (Arte and France 5, free on arte.tv and france.tv); the comedy archive: *Les Inconnus*, *Les Nuls*, *Les Guignols de l'info*, *Groland*, *Les Deschiens* (INA, YouTube); Devos, Coluche, Desproges | Daily show, debate and the comedy block |
| FRANCO | Québec: Radio-Canada's *Tout le monde en parle*, *District 31*, *Infoman*; Belgium: RTBF, *La Trêve*; Switzerland: RTS; Africa: RFI Afrique, TV5Monde's *Journal Afrique*, Mabanckou's Collège de France lectures; the Antilles: Chamoiseau and créole basics | Varieties for recognition, Blocks 2 and 3 |
| GALLICA | gallica.bnf.fr: the 1694 *Dictionnaire de l'Académie*, a 17th-century Molière edition, the 1857 *Madame Bovary*, Hugo's manuscripts; fr.wikisource.org for clean texts | Language history |
| COMMUTE | The commute slot: the audiobooks of the current book (*Swann*, *Bovary*, *Le Rouge et le Noir*, *Germinal*, *Mémoires d'Hadrien*, *Au revoir là-haut*, the plays in Comédie-Française recordings, and litteratureaudio.com for the rest); *Affaires sensibles* on Mondays | Listening only, 30 minutes a day |
| FREE | Free reading, 30 minutes a day from a C1 list (Appendix K): Diderot, then Faye, Thúy, Echenoz, Le Tellier, de Kerangal, Mathieu, Laferrière, de Vigan, Mauvignier, Lafon, Diome, Claudel, Énard | The living novel, 30 minutes a day |
| POD | *Les Chemins de la philosophie*, *Le Cours de l'histoire*, *Concordance des temps* (France Culture); *Le Masque et la Plume*, *Boomerang*, *Affaires sensibles* (France Inter); *Répliques*; *La Grande Librairie* (France 5); *Les Grosses Têtes* (RTL); *Le Floodcast*; *Transfert*; Collège de France lectures (Compagnon on Proust, Mabanckou); the Académie's *discours de réception* | Listening rhythm, two or three steps a week |
| NEWS | *Le Monde* daily with *Le Monde des livres* (Thursdays); *Le Canard enchaîné* (Wednesdays); *Le Monde diplomatique* (monthly); *Philosophie Magazine*, *Lire Magazine littéraire*, *Le 1*; *Mediapart* or *Libération* as the second daily; *Pour la Science* or *La Recherche* | Reading rhythm, the Canard pattern and the magazine step |
| FILM | *La Règle du jeu, Les Enfants du paradis, Hiroshima mon amour, Le Mépris, Les Diaboliques, Pickpocket, Les Parapluies de Cherbourg, Mommy, L'Armée des ombres, Rosetta, Le Trou, Timbuktu, La Noire de..., Le Charme discret de la bourgeoisie, Un homme et une femme, Les Olympiades* (sixteen), plus *La Bataille d'Alger*, *Germinal*, *Les Liaisons dangereuses* and *Au revoir là-haut* beside their books | About one every two weeks, no subtitles |
| POEMS | Appendix J: 24 poems and 12 chansons | One a week, by heart |
| SHADOW | France Inter's morning (*le 7/9*, the 8 o'clock *journal*) for the daily shadowing; one Comédie-Française alexandrine scene a block for stage diction | Prosody, daily and in Week 26 |
| FREQ | A 10,000-word French frequency list: FrequencyWords `fr_50k.txt` (ranks 1–10,000) or Lonsdale and Le Bras's *A Frequency Dictionary of French* (Routledge) for the first 5000 | Coverage checks Weeks 9, 18 and 27 |
| ANKI / AI | Your deck (20 new cards a day, mostly collocations, register sets and connectors); AI seminar partner, editor, DALF examiner, debate partner, four-mode voice cycle | As before |
| TEST | The DALF C1 *exemple de sujet* with audio and *corrigés* (france-education-international.fr, delfdalf.fr); an EAF *commentaire* subject with the official *grille*; a bac philosophy subject with its *corrigé*; the Certificat Voltaire | Exit verification |

---

## The C1 day

Every study day: **the dailies, in the order of the table, then the current step**, the first unticked step in this file. One step is one sitting with one focus; a step too long for today is resumed tomorrow. The old weekly rhythm is now individual steps placed in order inside each week: nothing runs in the background except the dailies.

| Daily | Minutes | Starts at | Ends |
|---|---|---|---|
| Anki: all due cards, then 20 new (collocations, register sets, connectors) | 20 | A1-004 | never |
| Commute slot: the audiobook of the current book; on Mondays *Affaires sensibles* | 30 | B2 | C2 changes its content |
| *Le Monde*, two articles: the lead story and one *Idées* column; *Le Monde des livres* on Thursdays; two harvested cards | 20 | B2 | never |
| AI voice, 15 minutes, in the four-mode cycle fixed by C1-008 | 15 | A2-009 | never |
| The daily poem or song, recited twice | 5 | C1-010 | never |
| *Journal de lecture*, 100–150 words with one imitated sentence, corrected on Sundays | 15 | C1-009 | never |
| Free reading, 30 minutes, from the fixed list in Appendix K | 30 | A2-135 | never |
| Shadowing of a France Inter presenter, one minute three times | 5 | C1-011 | never |

A week check passes when every step since the previous check is ticked and the dailies were done on at least six of the last seven days.

The steps that return every week are written as one line and use these patterns:

- **Long programme pattern**: the newest episode of the programme named (or the episode named), extensive, no pausing; then an 8-sentence French summary of its argument, not of its facts, in `writing/radio-C1.md`; one card for a turn of phrase you would not have produced.
- **Canard pattern**: this week's *Le Canard enchaîné* (Wednesday): the front page, *La Mare aux canards* and one inside page in full. List ten *jeux de mots* from headlines and captions; for each, write the idiom, proverb, title or name it twists and what the twist says; 5 cards. On the weeks that name a magazine as well, read two articles of it in the same sitting with three terms to Anki.
- **Exposé pattern**: 12 minutes on the topic named (Appendix F part 2), Recording protocol, with a one-page written plan (*introduction, problématique, deux ou trois parties, conclusion*); transcribed and corrected; the current poem or song recited at the end of the recording.
- **Debate pattern**: watch the newest *28 minutes* debate segment (Arte, about 20 minutes; *C ce soir* when *28 minutes* is off air) without subtitles, then a 20-minute AI debate on its question with the debate instruction of C1-005, you taking the side you agree with less; the last five minutes transcribed and corrected.
- **Poem or song pattern**: write out last week's from memory and mark the slips; then the next item of Appendix J: read it aloud three times, look up every word, scan the metre if it is in verse, learn the first half now. It is the daily poem or song until the next poem step.
- **Dictée pattern**: alternating weeks. Dictée weeks: a 150-to-200-word dictée (Pivot's dictées on the INA site and YouTube, *La Dictée d'Archibald* on TV5Monde, the Bescherelle or Projet Voltaire *dictées en ligne*), written, corrected against the text, error rate logged in `writing/dictees.md`, every error classified (agreement, homophone, accent, lexical). Projet Voltaire weeks: a 30-minute session on the next modules of the *Certificat Voltaire* path, score logged.
- **Reading-aloud pattern**: 3 minutes of the current book, recorded and compared with the audiobook or the Comédie-Française recording where one exists; three places where your rhythm, liaison or vowel length differed, repeated ten times.
- **Lagarde et Michard pattern**: the chapter named: the introduction and the author's pages; two extracts read aloud; the questions printed under one extract answered in five sentences in the notebook; the author's French Wikipedia entry for what the 1960s anthology leaves out; a row in the tracker's Canon table (author, work, century, LM chapter read, text written). Where *La Grande Librairie* or France Culture has an episode on the author, 15 minutes of it at the end.
- **Daily-show pattern**: one full *Quotidien* (TMC, on tf1.fr or YouTube; the newest), no subtitles, no pausing; five jokes or *vannes* explained in French (what each relies on: a *jeu de mots*, a *référence*, a register drop) and five expressions of the register of people under forty noted; 5 cards.
- **Casual series pattern**: the episode or episodes named, no subtitles, no pausing; six sentences in French on the plot and one on a feature of the language (a register, a profession's vocabulary, a regional trait); 5 cards; tracker.
- **Francophonie pattern**: the programme named, no subtitles; list ten features you noticed (lexicon, phonology, syntax, forms of address) and check each in the *Banque de dépannage linguistique*, the French Wikipedia article on the variety or the reference named in the step; 5 cards; two sentences in the tracker's Varieties table.
- **Film pattern**: no subtitles; a French review (*Télérama*, *Le Monde*, *Les Cahiers du cinéma* or *Positif*) read first; the film without pausing; 250 words in French on the question the step asks; tracker.
- **Comedy archive pattern** (Block 4): 45 minutes of the troupe or comedian named (INA's channels on YouTube, madelen.ina.fr, or the DVDs); ten jokes explained in French (register, reference, wordplay); five expressions that entered the language, checked in *Le Petit Robert*; 5 cards.
- **QSJ pattern**: the *Que sais-je ?* volume named, read in two sittings (about 64 pages each) with at most ten lookups per sitting and a half-page French summary of its argument after the second; then the France Culture programme named on the same theme, in the commute slot the next day, with two things it said that the book did not written in the tracker.

**Book pattern** (C1 form): (1) before: the *Lagarde et Michard* pages on the author (if not already read in the week's LM step), the introduction of your edition (Folio classique, GF Flammarion and Le Livre de Poche *classiques* have long scholarly ones; read it, including the notes on the text), the author's French Wikipedia entry in full; (2) during: the audiobook chapter first where one exists, in the commute slot; intensive on the first 20 pages, extensive after that with at most 6 lookups per 30 pages, marking in pencil every sentence whose construction you could not have produced; (3) after: a 500-word *commentaire composé* on a passage of your choice (EAF form, see C1-014) or, for the essays and the memoirs, a 500-word *essai* on one question the book raises; an editor pass with the instruction of C1-005; a 10-minute AI seminar on the book; the five marked sentences copied into the notebook and imitated with your own content; and the *Le Masque et la Plume*, *La Compagnie des œuvres* (France Culture) or *La Grande Librairie* episode on the book or author where one exists, heard in the commute slot after your text is written, never before.

**Episode pattern** (C1 form, *Le Bureau des légendes*): no subtitles, no pausing; one 10-minute scene rewatched with French subtitles as a check only; eight sentences in French on the plot and two on language (the intelligence service's jargon, a register shift, an idiom); 10 cards; tracker.

**Language-study pattern**: read the section named in BU or GPP; write every rule you did not already apply into the notebook in French, with the example; do the GPP exercises of the chapter where one is named and check them against the *corrigés*; then either write 20 own sentences on the point or apply it to three of your portfolio texts and note what changed. **EAF pattern**: a *commentaire composé* or a *dissertation* on the subject named, 2 hours at the desk with the text and a dictionary only, in the method of C1-014 and C1-111; then marked by yourself against the official *grille* (eduscol), then an editor pass; the clean copy with its error count to `writing/portfolio-C1.md`.

## Block 0: C1 setup (Week 1, days 1–2)

- [ ] **C1-001** [Setup · Focused · 30 min] Files and tracker.
      Do: notebook section C1; `writing/portfolio-C1.md`; `writing/journal-de-lecture.md`; `writing/radio-C1.md`; `writing/dictees.md` continues. In `canon-tracker.md` add a "Canon" table with columns author, work, century, LM chapter read, text written (*commentaire* or *essai*), and a "Varieties" table with columns variety, programme, features noticed. Reread the README methods and Appendix A here.
      Done when: files exist, Appendix A read.
- [ ] **C1-002** [Setup · Focused · 45 min] Reference works.
      Do: buy *Le Bon Usage* (Grevisse and Goosse, De Boeck Supérieur; the current edition, or the 16th second-hand; it is a 1700-page reference, not a course), *Grammaire progressive du français, niveau perfectionnement* with its *corrigés*, and *Le Petit Robert* (paper, or the app subscription). Get a *Français 1re* textbook (Nathan, Hatier or Magnard, any edition from 2019 on, second-hand; it has the *commentaire*, the *dissertation*, the figures, the versification and the *parcours*) and a *Terminale* philosophy manual (any big publisher, second-hand). Buy the six *Lagarde et Michard* (Bordas; second-hand sets cost a few tens of euros; the single-volume abridgement is not enough). Bookmark cnrtl.fr (the TLFi), academie-francaise.fr/dire-ne-pas-dire, bdl.oqlf.gouv.qc.ca, gallica.bnf.fr, fr.wikisource.org, eduscol.education.fr (search "EAF sujets"), litteratureaudio.com, projet-voltaire.fr, and the DALF C1 page of france-education-international.fr. Install Antidote if you can afford it (it marks agreement, register and anglicisms; the editor instruction replaces it otherwise).
      Done when: books in hand or ordered, bookmarks made.
- [ ] **C1-003** [Setup · Focused · 20 min] Books for the first ten weeks.
      Do: get *Du côté de chez Swann* (Folio classique, with Antoine Compagnon's introduction, or GF) and its audiobook (André Dussollier's reading for Thélème is the reference; litteratureaudio.com has a free one), *La Chute* (Folio), *Exercices de style* (Folio), *Les Mots* (Folio), *Les Années* (Folio) and *Madame Bovary* (Folio classique or GF, with the audiobook). Reserve the rest from the resource table as you go; Appendix L has editions.
      Done when: in hand.
- [ ] **C1-004** [Setup · Focused · 30 min] Press, podcasts and television.
      Do: keep *Le Monde* and add the Thursday *Le Monde des livres* to your routine; subscribe to *Le Canard enchaîné* (paper or the digital edition, about 8 euros a month; it has no website worth the name, which is the point) and to *Le Monde diplomatique* (digital, about 5 euros a month); bookmark *Philosophie Magazine*, *Lire Magazine littéraire*, *Le 1* (le1hebdo.fr) and *Pour la Science*; choose *Mediapart* or *Libération* as the second daily. In your podcast app add *Les Chemins de la philosophie*, *Le Cours de l'histoire*, *Concordance des temps*, *Le Masque et la Plume*, *Boomerang*, *Affaires sensibles*, *Répliques*, *Les Grosses Têtes*, *Le Floodcast*, *Transfert* and the Collège de France feed (Antoine Compagnon's *Proust* lectures of 2006–2007 and Alain Mabanckou's 2016 chair). Find the television: *Le Bureau des légendes* seasons 2–5 (Canal+ in France; abroad on the platform in Appendix L); *En thérapie* S1 on arte.tv; *Hippocrate* S1, *Les Sauvages*, *Engrenages* S2 and *Baron noir* S1 on Canal+ or where Appendix L says; *La Trêve* on Netflix or RTBF Auvio; *Quotidien* on tf1.fr; *28 minutes* on arte.tv; *C ce soir* on france.tv; Radio-Canada's *Tout le monde en parle* and *Infoman* on ici.radio-canada.ca and YouTube, *District 31* on ICI Tou.tv (clips on YouTube where it is blocked abroad).
      Done when: subscriptions active, titles located.
- [ ] **C1-005** [Setup · Focused · 15 min] AI instructions.
      Do: save four instructions. Seminar: « Tu es un camarade de khâgne. Nous discutons du texte de la semaine. Conteste mes affirmations, exige des citations et des preuves, utilise un registre soutenu. Liste mes erreurs à la fin, en insistant sur le lexique, les collocations et le registre, pas seulement sur la grammaire. » Editor: « Tu es secrétaire de rédaction au *Monde*. Corrige mon texte : langue, orthographe (y compris les accords et la typographie française), style, cohésion, argumentation ; propose des coupes ; montre-moi une version publiable. » Examiner: « Tu es examinateur du DALF C1. Pose-moi des questions de niveau universitaire sur mon exposé et demande-moi de nuancer, d'argumenter et de relier à d'autres textes ; à la fin, note-moi sur la grille officielle. » Debate: « Tu es mon contradicteur dans un débat à la française : tu défends la position opposée avec vigueur, tu m'interromps poliment, tu exiges des exemples. » Anki: 20 new cards a day; from now on most cards are collocations, connectors and register sets (front: a plain sentence; back: the same idea in *soutenu*, *courant* and *familier*).
      Done when: four instructions saved.
- [ ] **C1-006** [Habit · Casual · 30 min/day] **The commute slot at C1.**
      Do: listening only, every study day, with fixed content. Mondays: the newest *Affaires sensibles* (France Inter, 55 minutes; finish it on Tuesday if needed). Every other day: the audiobook of the book the guide has you reading, always a chapter you have already read: *Du côté de chez Swann* (Weeks 1–6), *Madame Bovary* (10–12), *Le Rouge et le Noir* (15–18), *Germinal* (19–25), *Mémoires d'Hadrien* (26–27), *Au revoir là-haut* (28–31) all have one; the plays have Comédie-Française recordings; the public-domain books not named have a free reading on litteratureaudio.com. When the current book has none, the audiobook of your current free-reading novel (C1-007) where one exists, and otherwise the day's *Les Chemins de la philosophie*. On the day after you finish a book, its *Masque et la Plume* or *Compagnie des œuvres* episode takes the slot (book pattern). Log it in the margin.
      Québec option: on Mondays, Radio-Canada's *Aujourd'hui l'histoire* instead of *Affaires sensibles* every second week.
      Done when: today's slot is filled.
- [ ] **C1-007** [Habit · Casual · 30 min/day] **Free reading at C1: the living novel.**
      Do: the daily continues, 30 minutes, and the list is deliberately where the twenty-eight C1 works are not: the French and Francophone novel of the last twenty years, after one eighteenth-century exception. It is fixed; read it in this order, one book at a time (finish the B2 list first if a book of it is still open): (1) Diderot, *Jacques le fataliste et son maître*; (2) Gaël Faye, *Petit Pays*; (3) Kim Thúy, *Ru*; (4) Jean Echenoz, *14*; (5) Hervé Le Tellier, *L'Anomalie*; (6) Maylis de Kerangal, *Réparer les vivants*; (7) Nicolas Mathieu, *Leurs enfants après eux*; (8) Dany Laferrière, *L'Énigme du retour*; (9) Delphine de Vigan, *Rien ne s'oppose à la nuit*; (10) Laurent Mauvignier, *Des hommes*; (11) Marie-Hélène Lafon, *Histoire du fils*; (12) Fatou Diome, *Le Ventre de l'Atlantique*; (13) Philippe Claudel, *Les Âmes grises*; (14) Mathias Énard, *Rue des voleurs*. No dictionary, no cards, no *commentaire*: this is the reading you do for yourself. Log pages; finished titles in the tracker. You will finish about seven at C1; the rest carry over.
      Done when: first session logged today.
- [ ] **C1-008** [Habit · Focused · 15 min/day] **AI voice at C1: the four-mode cycle.**
      Do: the daily session continues at 15 minutes, now in a four-day cycle with the instructions of C1-005. Day 1, seminar: the pages of the current book you read yesterday. Day 2, debate: the week's exposé or debate topic, you on the side you like less. Day 3, examiner: a DALF-style question on this week's radio step, answered in a 5-minute exposé and questioned. Day 4, register: tell the same short story (something from your day) three times, in *registre soutenu*, *courant* and *familier*, and ask the assistant to name every form that belonged to the wrong register. Then day 1 again.
      Done when: today's session is done.
- [ ] **C1-009** [Habit · Focused · 15 min/day] **Journal de lecture.**
      Do: the journal becomes a reading journal: every study day, 100 to 150 words in `writing/journal-de-lecture.md` on what you read that day (the current book, the free-reading novel or *Le Monde*), with one quotation, one observation about the language, and one sentence of your own that imitates the construction of a sentence you marked in pencil. Corrected in one batch on Sundays with the editor instruction; the corrected sentences copied by hand.
      Done when: today's entry is written.
- [ ] **C1-010** [Habit · Casual · 5 min/day] **The daily poem or song.**
      Do: the daily song becomes the daily poem or song from Appendix J: recite or sing the current one aloud twice every day, from memory as soon as you can, with the *e muets* of verse pronounced and the liaisons made. Each poem step tests the old one and gives you the next.
      Done when: recited today.
- [ ] **C1-011** [Habit · Casual · 5 min/day] **Shadowing a France Inter presenter.**
      Do: every study day, one minute of that morning's 8 o'clock *journal* on France Inter (the *7/9*; on radiofrance.fr or the app, available all day): listen once, then speak along with it three times without pausing, imitating the presenter's rhythm, the rising continuation tones, the falling finals, the *accent d'insistance* on the first syllable of key words (*c'est une CAtastrophe*), the liaisons and the *e caducs* dropped. Once a week (on the day of the reading-aloud step) record the third pass and listen back.
      Québec option: Radio-Canada's *Téléjournal* presenter on alternate days, to hear both norms.
      Done when: shadowed today.
- [ ] **C1-012** [Produce · Focused · 20 min] C1 baseline recording.
      Do: Recording protocol, 10 minutes: « Qu'est-ce que la littérature française m'a appris jusqu'ici sur la France, et qu'est-ce que j'attends de l'année qui vient ? » Keep for the then-and-now steps of the exit check.
      Done when: `YYYY-MM-DD-C1-012.m4a` saved.
- [ ] **C1-013** [Check] Setup check. Pass if C1-001 to C1-012 are ticked.

---

## Block 1: Weeks 1–9, the twentieth century and Proust

### Week 1

- [ ] **C1-014** [Study · Deep · 90 min] EAF, the *commentaire composé* in full.
      Do: read your *Français 1re* textbook's method pages on the *commentaire* (every publisher has them under *Méthode* or *Vers le bac*) and the official description on eduscol. Write the skeleton into the notebook: (1) *introduction*: an *amorce* (the movement, the author, the work), the *présentation du texte* (where the passage sits, what happens), the *problématique* (one question the passage answers: « Comment ce passage fait-il de l'attente un drame ? »), the *annonce du plan*; (2) two or three *axes de lecture* (*parties*), each with two or three *sous-parties*, each sub-part being one idea supported by two or three *procédés* (a figure, a tense, a rhythm, a register, a sound) quoted exactly and *analysed*, never paraphrased: « l'anaphore de *longtemps* ... souligne ... »; a *transition* between parts; (3) a *conclusion* that answers the *problématique* and opens (*ouverture*) on another text or the work as a whole. The marking: the *grille* on eduscol weights *compréhension et interprétation*, *organisation*, *expression* (the written language); a 4-hour exam at the bac, 2 hours in this guide. Then read two *commentaires rédigés* from the past papers' *corrigés* (eduscol or the académie sites; annabac.com has free ones) and mark in them the *problématique*, the parts and ten *procédés*. Finally, in 30 minutes, write the introduction and one sub-part on the opening page of *Du côté de chez Swann* (« Longtemps, je me suis couché de bonne heure »), from your own first reading of it (the Proust sheet of the next steps will deepen it).
      Done when: skeleton in the notebook, two *corrigés* marked, one introduction and one sub-part written and filed.
- [ ] **C1-015** [Drill · Casual · 30 min] Poem or song 1 (Appendix J): Villon, *Ballade des pendus* (poem or song pattern).
- [ ] **C1-016** [Culture · Deep · 90 min] *Lagarde et Michard*, orientation.
      Do: open the *XXe siècle* volume: read its introduction and its table of contents, and skim the five other volumes' tables of contents. In the tracker's Canon table, enter every author named in the resource table with century and LM chapter. Then read the chapter on Proust in full (the biography, the pages on the *Recherche*, the extracts with their notes), two extracts aloud, the questions under one extract answered in five sentences.
      Done when: table filled, Proust chapter read, five sentences written.
- [ ] **C1-017** [Culture · Focused · 60 min] Proust, before reading.
      Do: the French Wikipedia entry « Marcel Proust » in full and « À la recherche du temps perdu » to the end of the section on the structure of the seven volumes; your edition's introduction (Compagnon's in Folio classique) and its *note sur le texte*; the *Pléiade* genesis in two sentences (the 1913 self-financed Grasset edition, the Goncourt of 1919 for *À l'ombre des jeunes filles en fleurs*). Write a one-page sheet: the narrator against the author; *Combray*, *Un amour de Swann*, *Noms de pays : le nom*; the *drame du coucher*, the *madeleine*, the two *côtés*; and the first sentence copied by hand with its tenses named (*passé composé* with an adverb of duration, then the *imparfait* of habit, then the *plus-que-parfait*). Listen to the first ten minutes of the Dussollier audiobook with the text.
      Done when: sheet written.

      Done when: this sitting's share is done (about a third of the whole); stop at a natural break.

The Canard's language is a register of its own: every headline is a pun on an idiom, a title or a name, and the *brèves* assume you know last week's news. Then the pattern.

Your summary names each critic's verdict and one adjective each used; the vocabulary of judgement (*roboratif, poussif, convenu, ciselé, besogneux, jubilatoire*) goes to Anki, five cards a week.

Two episodes a step, one step a week to Week 18. Note how each patient's register marks them.

### Week 2

- [ ] **C1-033** [Study · Deep · 90 min] The *passé simple* and the *passé antérieur*, produced.
      Do: BU, the chapters on the *passé simple* (its forms and its use) and GPP, the chapter on the *temps du récit* (language-study pattern, with the GPP exercises). You have read the *passé simple* since B1; from this week you write it. The forms, into the notebook in full: first group *aimer*: *j'aimai, tu aimas, il aima, nous aimâmes, vous aimâtes, ils aimèrent*; second group and most third *finir*: *je finis, tu finis, il finit, nous finîmes, vous finîtes, ils finirent*; the *-u-* group *recevoir*: *je reçus, tu reçus, il reçut, nous reçûmes, vous reçûtes, ils reçurent*; *venir*/*tenir*: *je vins, tu vins, il vint, nous vînmes, vous vîntes, ils vinrent*. The forty irregular stems you must own: *avoir* j'eus, *être* je fus, *faire* je fis, *dire* je dis, *voir* je vis, *prendre* je pris, *mettre* je mis, *écrire* j'écrivis, *lire* je lus, *croire* je crus, *savoir* je sus, *pouvoir* je pus, *vouloir* je voulus, *devoir* je dus, *boire* je bus, *vivre* je vécus, *mourir* je mourus, *naître* je naquis, *connaître* je connus, *paraître* je parus, *plaire* je plus, *taire* je tus, *courir* je courus, *conclure* je conclus, *résoudre* je résolus, *craindre* je craignis, *peindre* je peignis, *joindre* je joignis, *vaincre* je vainquis, *coudre* je cousis, *suivre* je suivis, *rire* je ris, *conduire* je conduisis, *valoir* je valus, *falloir* il fallut, *pleuvoir* il plut, *s'asseoir* je m'assis, *acquérir* j'acquis, *offrir* j'offris, *cueillir* je cueillis. The circumflex in *nous* and *vous* only. The *passé antérieur* (*quand il eut fini, il sortit*; after *dès que, aussitôt que, à peine ... que*), the literary pairing *passé simple* for the foreground, *imparfait* for the background, and the rule of modern prose: the *passé simple* lives in the third person of written narrative (the novel, the press's *récit*, history) and nowhere in speech; *je fus* and *nous allâmes* read as parody or Proust. Then write a 300-word *récit* in the *passé simple* (something that happened to you last year, told as a novelist would, third person), editor pass.
      Done when: tables in the notebook, GPP exercises checked, the *récit* corrected.

### Week 3

- [ ] **C1-053** [Study · Deep · 90 min] The *subjonctif imparfait* and *plus-que-parfait*, and the *concordance des temps* in full.
      Do: BU, the chapters on the *subjonctif* (forms, the literary tenses, the *concordance*) and GPP, the chapter on the *concordance des temps* (language-study pattern, with the exercises). The forms, built on the *passé simple*: *que j'aimasse, que tu aimasses, qu'il aimât, que nous aimassions, que vous aimassiez, qu'ils aimassent*; *que je finisse, qu'il finît*; *que je reçusse, qu'il reçût*; *que je fusse, qu'il fût*; *que j'eusse, qu'il eût*; *que je vinsse, qu'il vînt*; the circumflex on the third person singular only, which distinguishes *qu'il fût* from *il fut* and *qu'il eût fini* (*plus-que-parfait du subjonctif*) from *il eut fini*. The rule of the *concordance* in literary prose: main verb in a past tense, subordinate subjunctive in the imperfect for simultaneity or posteriority (*il voulait qu'elle vînt*) and the pluperfect for anteriority (*il regrettait qu'elle fût partie*); the rule of modern prose and all speech: present and past subjunctive regardless (*il voulait qu'elle vienne*). What survives: the third person singular (*fût, eût, vînt, pût, fît, allât*) in serious written French, and the frozen *fût-ce*, *ne fût-ce que*, *dût-il*, *eût-il*, *plût au ciel*; the first and second persons and the plurals are archaic or comic (*que vous fussiez* is Molière or a joke). The *conditionnel passé deuxième forme* (*il eût voulu* = *il aurait voulu*) in Proust and Flaubert. Then the whole indicative *concordance* in a table: main in present or future → subordinate present / passé composé / future; main in past → *imparfait* for simultaneity, *plus-que-parfait* for anteriority, *conditionnel* (the *futur dans le passé*) and *conditionnel passé* for posteriority; reported speech shifts (*il a dit qu'il viendrait*, *qu'il était venu*, *qu'il venait*); *si* clauses in the three forms, and the literary *si* + *plus-que-parfait du subjonctif* (*s'il eût su*). Then rewrite the 300-word *récit* of C1-033 with four subordinates in the literary *concordance* and mark the *fût*/*fut* pairs.
      Done when: tables in the notebook, exercises checked, *récit* rewritten and corrected.

### Week 4

- [ ] **C1-072** [Study · Deep · 90 min] Agreement in every case, part 1: the *participe passé*.
      Do: BU, the chapters on the agreement of the past participle, and GPP, its chapter on the *accord du participe passé* (language-study pattern, with the exercises). The rules, written out with an example each: (1) with *être*, with the subject (*elles sont parties*); (2) with *avoir*, with the direct object when it precedes (*les lettres que j'ai écrites*; *je les ai vues*; *combien en as-tu lu ?* no agreement with *en*; *quelle erreur a-t-il faite ?*); (3) never with an indirect object or with the subject (*elle a téléphoné*, *ils se sont succédé*, *elles se sont parlé*, *elle s'est plu à Paris*); (4) pronominal verbs: agreement with the reflexive pronoun when it is a direct object (*elle s'est lavée*) and none when a direct object follows (*elle s'est lavé les mains*), always for the essentially pronominal (*elles se sont souvenues*, *ils se sont évanouis*) except *s'arroger*; (5) followed by an infinitive: agreement if the preceding object does the action of the infinitive (*les enfants que j'ai vus jouer*) and none if it undergoes it (*la chanson que j'ai entendu chanter*); *fait* + infinitive invariable (*elle s'est fait couper les cheveux*), *laissé* + infinitive invariable in the 1990 rules and variable by the old rule; (6) *coûté, valu, pesé, couru, vécu, dormi* invariable in their measure sense (*les trois heures que j'ai dormi*) and variable in the transitive one (*les efforts que cela m'a coûtés*); (7) impersonal and *il y a*: invariable (*la chaleur qu'il a fait*); (8) *ci-joint, ci-inclus, vu, attendu, excepté, passé, y compris* invariable before the noun, variable after (*ci-joint les documents* / *les documents ci-joints*); (9) *l'* standing for a clause: invariable (*plus difficile que je ne l'avais cru*); (10) *dû, cru, pu, voulu* with an understood infinitive: invariable (*tous les efforts qu'il a pu*). Then the GPP exercises, and 30 sentences of your own covering every rule, editor pass.
      Done when: ten rules with examples in the notebook, exercises checked, 30 sentences corrected.

### Week 5

- [ ] **C1-091** [Study · Deep · 90 min] Agreement in every case, part 2: adjectives, numbers, collectives, *tout*, *même*, *quelque*.
      Do: BU, the chapters on the agreement of the adjective and on the numerals, and GPP's chapter on the *accords* (language-study pattern, with the exercises). Written out with an example each: (1) compound colour adjectives invariable (*des robes bleu foncé*, *des yeux gris-vert*) and the noun-colours invariable (*des chemises orange*, *des gants marron*) except *rose, mauve, écarlate, fauve, pourpre, violet*; (2) *demi* and *nu* invariable before the noun (*une demi-heure*, *nu-pieds*), variable after (*une heure et demie*, *pieds nus*); (3) *tout*: adverb and invariable before an adjective (*ils sont tout contents*) except before a feminine beginning with a consonant or *h aspiré* (*elle est toute contente*, *toutes honteuses*); *tout entière*; (4) *même*: variable as adjective (*les mêmes idées*, *eux-mêmes*), invariable as adverb (*même les enfants*); (5) *quelque*: invariable before a number (*quelque trois cents personnes*), variable otherwise (*quelques personnes*); *quel que* in two words before a verb (*quelles que soient vos raisons*); *quoique* against *quoi que*; (6) numbers: *vingt* and *cent* take *-s* when multiplied and not followed by another number (*quatre-vingts*, *trois cents*, but *quatre-vingt-trois*, *trois cent dix*); *mille* invariable; *million* and *milliard* are nouns; (7) collective subjects: *la plupart des gens sont*, *un grand nombre de* with either, *la moitié des élèves est* or *sont* (sense), *plus d'un est*, *moins de deux sont*; *ni l'un ni l'autre n'est venu* or *ne sont venus*; (8) *c'est* / *ce sont* before a plural (*ce sont eux*, with *c'est eux* accepted in speech); (9) *possible* invariable after *le plus / le moins* (*le plus de livres possible*); (10) *avoir l'air*: *elle a l'air contente* (with *air* literal: *elle a l'air content*); (11) the adjective after several nouns in the masculine plural, and the proximity agreement (*un courage et une force admirables*); (12) *des plus*: *un homme des plus honnêtes*; *feu la reine* but *la feue reine*; *ci-annexé*. Then the GPP exercises and 30 sentences, editor pass.
      Done when: twelve rules in the notebook, exercises checked, 30 sentences corrected.

### Week 6

- [ ] **C1-111** [Study · Deep · 90 min] EAF, the *dissertation* and the rhetoric of argument.
      Do: your *Français 1re* textbook's method pages on the *dissertation* and eduscol's description. Into the notebook: the *sujet* is a quotation or a question on an *œuvre au programme* (« Le roman doit-il donner l'illusion du réel ? »); the *analyse du sujet* (every term defined, the presupposition found); the *problématique* (the tension the subject hides, as one question); the *plan*: *dialectique* (*thèse*, *antithèse*, *synthèse* or *dépassement*, never a flat « oui, non, peut-être ») or *thématique* (three aspects); each part two or three *arguments*, each argument one *exemple* précis from a text (title, author, scene, a quotation); the *introduction* (*amorce*, *sujet cité*, *problématique*, *annonce du plan*), the *transitions*, the *conclusion* (answer, then *ouverture*). The rhetoric you need: the *connecteurs logiques* by function (addition: *de plus, en outre, par ailleurs*; opposition: *or, toutefois, néanmoins, en revanche, pour autant*; concession: *certes ... mais*, *s'il est vrai que*, *quoique*; cause: *dans la mesure où, puisque*; consequence: *dès lors, partant, de sorte que, aussi* + inversion; illustration: *à cet égard, en témoigne, ainsi*; restriction: *du moins, encore faut-il que*), the *modalisateurs* (*sans doute, il semble que, on peut penser que, force est de constater*), *ethos, pathos, logos*, the *question rhétorique*, the *concession* as the move that makes a French essay sound educated. Read one *corrigé* of a *dissertation* from the past papers and mark its *problématique*, its three parts and its transitions. Then write the complete plan (introduction written out, parts and arguments as a tree, conclusion written out) for the subject above, using *Swann* and *La Règle du jeu*.
      Done when: method in the notebook, *corrigé* marked, plan written and corrected.

### Week 7

- [ ] **C1-130** [Study · Deep · 90 min] The registers in full.
      Do: BU's introductory pages on *les niveaux de langue* and *Le Petit Robert*'s guide to its labels (*littér., sout., cour., fam., pop., vulg., arg., vieilli, vx, région., didact., péj.*); GPP's chapter on the *registres* (language-study pattern). Build a table in the notebook with seven columns, *soutenu / littéraire / courant / familier / populaire / argot / verlan* and *le langage des jeunes*, and thirty rows: *voiture* (*automobile, voiture, bagnole, caisse*), *argent* (*fonds, argent, fric, thune, oseille, pognon, blé*), *manger* (*se restaurer, manger, bouffer, grailler*), *travail* (*labeur, travail, boulot, taf*), *femme* (*dame, femme, nana, meuf, gonzesse*), *homme* (*monsieur, homme, mec, type, keum*), *fou* (*dément, fou, dingue, ouf*), *fête* (*réception, fête, teuf*), *policier* (*agent, policier, flic, keuf, condé*), *enfant* (*gamin, gosse, môme*), *dormir* (*pioncer, roupiller*), *comprendre* (*piger, capter*), *partir* (*se retirer, partir, se barrer, se casser, se tirer*), *beaucoup* (*fort, beaucoup, vachement, grave*), *ennuyeux* (*fastidieux, ennuyeux, chiant, relou*), *bizarre* (*singulier, bizarre, zarbi, chelou*), *fatigué* (*las, fatigué, crevé, claqué, vénère* for angry), *vêtements* (*fringues, sapes*), *livre* (*ouvrage, livre, bouquin*), *cigarette* (*clope*), *rien* (*que dalle*), *peur* (*la trouille, les jetons*), *se tromper* (*se gourer, se planter*), *énervé* (*irrité, énervé, vénère*), *aimer* (*kiffer*), *merci* (*cimer*), *frère* (*reuf, frérot*), *mère* (*reum, daronne*), *regarder* (*mater*), *embrasser* (*pécho* for picking up). Then the grammatical markers of each: *ne* kept or dropped, *nous* / *on*, the four question forms (*Où allez-vous ?* / *Où est-ce que tu vas ?* / *Tu vas où ?* / *Où tu vas ?*), *cela* / *ça*, the dislocation (*moi, je ...*; *il est où, le chat ?*), the *tu* / *vous*, the literary *l'on* and *ne* alone, the *passé simple* against the *passé composé*, the inversion against intonation. Then take one paragraph of this week's *Quotidien* transcript (write it from the audio), rewrite it in *registre soutenu*, and one paragraph of *Le Monde*'s editorial rewritten in *familier*; editor pass on both, asking it to flag every word that stayed in the wrong register.
      Done when: the table (30 rows) and the markers are in the notebook, two rewrites corrected.

### Week 8

- [ ] **C1-149** [Study · Deep · 90 min] The 1990 *rectifications de l'orthographe* beside the traditional spelling.
      Do: read the *rapport du Conseil supérieur de la langue française* summary on the Académie's site (search « rectifications de l'orthographe 1990 ») and the Office québécois's *BDL* pages on the same (the BDL applies them; the Académie « recommends » them without imposing; French school programmes since 2016 teach them as the reference; most books, newspapers and Projet Voltaire still print the traditional spelling). Into the notebook, both columns: (1) numbers hyphenated throughout (*vingt-et-un*, *deux-cents*, *trois-millions*) against *vingt et un*, *deux cents*; (2) the circumflex dropped on *i* and *u* (*il parait*, *la flute*, *le gout*, *il plait*, *aout*) except where it distinguishes (*dû, mûr, sûr, jeûne*, the *passé simple* and the *subjonctif imparfait* forms *nous fûmes, qu'il fût*); (3) *-eler* and *-eter* verbs with *è* (*elle ruissèle*, *il époussète*) except *appeler*, *jeter* and their compounds; (4) the accent moved to match pronunciation (*évènement*, *règlementaire*, *cèleri*, *crèmerie*, *je cèderai* instead of *je céderai*); (5) the participle *laissé* invariable before an infinitive (*elle s'est laissé convaincre*); (6) compounds soldered (*portemonnaie*, *weekend*, *tictac*, *chauvesouris*) and compound plurals regularised (*des après-midis*, *un essuie-main, des essuie-mains*); (7) *-illier* to *-iller* where the *i* is silent (*joailler*, *serpillère*); (8) borrowings accented and pluralised as French (*un révolver*, *des sandwichs*, *un diésel*, *à capella*); (9) the *tréma* moved (*aigüe*, *ambigüe*, *argüer*); (10) the anomalies (*ognon*, *nénufar*, *bonhommie*, *charriot*, *assoir*, *relai*, *douçâtre*). Decide: you write the traditional spelling (what the Certificat Voltaire, *Le Monde* and your editions use) and you recognise the rectified one without flinching in Québec texts and in schoolbooks. Then a 100-word dictée of your own choosing from a Québec newspaper (*Le Devoir* applies the rectifications) written twice, once each way.
      Done when: the ten rules in two columns, the double dictée done.

Watch ten minutes of the video of the ceremony (the Académie's YouTube channel) for the *habit vert* and the diction.
      Done when: ten sentences copied, formulas noted.

### Week 9: Milestone C1.1

- [ ] **C1-169** [Study · Deep · 90 min] The typography of French.
      Do: read the French Wikipedia entries « Espace insécable », « Guillemet » and « Usage des majuscules en français », and, if you can find it second-hand, the *Lexique des règles typographiques en usage à l'Imprimerie nationale* (the reference every French publisher uses); otherwise the typography pages of the BDL. Into the notebook, with an example each: (1) a thin or non-breaking space before *; : ! ?* and inside « guillemets », none before a comma or full stop (*Vraiment ? Oui !*); (2) « guillemets français » for quotations with the incise inside them unmarked (« Je viens, dit-il, demain ») and “English” ones only for a quotation inside a quotation; (3) the *tiret cadratin* (the long dash, —) for dialogue lines and the *tiret demi-cadratin* (–) for parenthetical cuts, never the hyphen; (4) capitals: *la Seconde Guerre mondiale*, *la Révolution française*, *le ministre de l'Éducation nationale*, *l'État*, *l'Église* as institution and *l'église* as building, *les Français* but *le peuple français* and *la langue française*, days and months in lower case (*lundi 3 mars*), *le président de la République*, *la Ve République*, titles of works with the capital on the first noun and the adjective that precedes it (*Le Rouge et le Noir*, *À la recherche du temps perdu*, *Les Liaisons dangereuses*); (5) accents kept on capitals (*À*, *État*, *Émile*); (6) italics for titles, foreign words and *a fortiori*, roman for titles in italics inside a title; (7) numbers: a non-breaking space as the thousands separator (*1 000 000*), the comma as the decimal (*3,5 %*, with a space before %), centuries in small capitals with superscript (*XVIIe siècle*), *1er*, *1re*, *2e* (never *2ème*); (8) abbreviations *M.*, *MM.*, *Mme*, *Mmes*, *Mlle*, *Dr*, *Me* (*maître*), *p.*, *cf.*, *etc.* with one full stop; *n°*; (9) the *trait d'union* in compound names and numbers; *c'est-à-dire*; *peut-être*; *au-dessus*; (10) *e* in *œ* (*œuvre*, *cœur*, *sœur*) and *æ* typed as ligatures. Set your keyboard for *œ*, *É*, *«* and the non-breaking space (on Windows Alt+0156, Alt+0201, Alt+0171 and Ctrl+Shift+Space in Word; on a Mac Option-q, the *Caps Lock* trick, Option-7; or install a *clavier français enrichi* like the Bépo or the *AZERTY NF Z71-300*). Then retype one page of your portfolio to these rules and run it through the editor instruction asking for typography only.
      Done when: ten rules in the notebook, keyboard set, one page retyped.

      Pass if all of:
      1. **Vocabulary**: Anki at least 9500 cards, 6500 mature, retention 85 percent or above; FREQ check 1 at 800 of 1000 or better.
      2. **Language**: Appendix H part 1 (12 items), at least 10 correct (marking: any error of grammar, agreement, register, idiom, spelling or typography counts).
      3. **Listening**: a *Répliques* episode you have not heard: written summary of each guest's position and the host's, at least 8 of 10 points on a second listen; a *Le Bureau des légendes* episode you have not seen (S2E10), whole, no subtitles: 12 facts, 10 right when checked with subtitles.
      4. **Reading**: a *Le Monde diplomatique* article of 2000 words in 15 minutes, one pass: summary of thesis and structure, no factual error, the writer's tone named in one sentence and justified with two quotations.
      5. **Speaking**: presentation 1 at most 4 errors in the exposé; the examiner's list at most 6; the voice-session transcripts of the week show at least five *connecteurs* of the *registre soutenu* (*en outre, dès lors, néanmoins, en revanche, à cet égard*) used naturally.
      6. **Writing**: Appendix I task 18 (a *commentaire composé* on the opening page of *Les Années*, 600 words), 90 minutes: at most 1.5 errors per 100 words, the EAF structure complete, at least ten *procédés* quoted and analysed; the dictée rate of the last two at or under 4 percent.
      7. **Spelling**: Projet Voltaire's own level test at *Supérieur* or above.
      8. **Habits**: nine weeks complete; QSJ 3; films 4; poems 9; *Swann*, *La Chute*, *Les Mots*, *Les Années* in the Canon table with their texts.
      Fail rules: 1 → Anki fortnight at 25 new cards, FREQ redone; 2 → the Study steps of the failed points redone (C1-033, C1-053, C1-072, C1-091, C1-130, C1-149, C1-169); 3 → LRS on four *Répliques* segments and four *Bureau* scenes; 4 → six *Diplo* articles intensively; 5 → daily seminar mode for two weeks; 6 → two more *commentaires* with the editor; 7 → four Projet Voltaire sessions.

## Block 2: Weeks 10–18, the nineteenth century and Québec

### Week 10

- [ ] **C1-188** [Study · Deep · 90 min] The syntax of literary prose: pronouns, *ne*, inversions.
      Do: BU, the chapters on the personal and relative pronouns, on *ne* and on the inversion of the subject; GPP, the chapters on the *pronoms* and on the *mise en relief* (language-study pattern, with the exercises). Into the notebook: (1) *dont* against *duquel / de laquelle* (*duquel* when the relative depends on a prepositional phrase: *la maison au fond de laquelle*; *dont* never after a preposition); *ce dont*, *ce à quoi*, *d'où*; *lequel* as a subject relative in administrative prose (*trois témoins, lesquels ont déclaré*); (2) *y* and *en* not used for persons in careful prose (*je pense à elle*, not *j'y pense*); *en* with the participle without agreement; (3) *l'on* after *si, que, où, et* in written French; (4) *ce* / *il* before *être* (*c'est un médecin* / *il est médecin*; *il est vrai que* / *c'est vrai*); (5) the *ne explétif* after *craindre que, avant que, à moins que, plus ... que* and in *je crains qu'il ne vienne* (no negation), and the literary *ne* alone with *cesser, oser, pouvoir, savoir* (*je ne saurais vous dire*, *il n'ose*); (6) the obligatory inversion in the *incise* (*dit-il*, *répondit-elle*, *s'écria le docteur*) and after fronted *aussi, ainsi, peut-être, à peine, sans doute, encore, du moins, en vain* (*Aussi est-il parti*; *Peut-être viendra-t-elle*; *À peine était-il sorti que*), the *-t-* (*viendra-t-il*), the complex inversion (*Peut-être Marie viendra-t-elle*); the inversion in relative and concessive clauses (*la maison qu'habitait mon oncle*; *quoi qu'en disent les critiques*); (7) *tel* agreement (*telle est ma conclusion*; *tel que* with the preceding noun); (8) the *mise en relief*: *c'est ... qui / que*, *ce qui ..., c'est*, the dislocation in speech; (9) the pronoun order with two objects in the imperative (*donne-le-moi*, *donne-m'en*); (10) *soi* for an indefinite subject only (*chacun pour soi*). Then take two pages of *Combray* and mark every relative, every *ne* and every inversion; write 20 sentences with the ten points; editor pass.
      Done when: ten points in the notebook, two pages marked, 20 sentences corrected.

      Do: the Québec block begins: nine weeks of Radio-Canada television in the Francophonie slot, with the Québec Study step in Week 17 and *Les Belles-sœurs* beside it. *Tout le monde en parle* (Sunday evening, Guy A. Lepage; on ici.radio-canada.ca/tele, with full programmes and segments on YouTube) is the Québec talk show: politicians, singers and athletes round one table for two hours, interviewed in the French of Montréal with the audience laughing at jokes you will not get at first. Before the first one, read the French Wikipedia entry « Français québécois » (phonology and lexicon sections) once, for orientation; the Study step will do it properly. Then the pattern: ten features (the affricated *tu* and *di*, the diphthongs in *père* and *fête*, *-tu* as a question particle, *là* as a filler, *c'est correct*, *chum*, *blonde*, *char*, *magasiner*, *dispendieux*, *présentement*, *la fin de semaine*, *bonjour* as goodbye, the anglicisms that France does not have and the ones it has that Québec refuses), checked in the BDL. In the tracker's Varieties table: two sentences.
      Done when: ten features checked, 5 cards, tracker.

### Week 11

- [ ] **C1-207** [Study · Deep · 90 min] The figures of speech in full.
      Do: your *Français 1re* textbook's pages on the *figures de style* (every edition has a *fiche* of two to four pages) and the French Wikipedia entry « Figure de style » for the ones it lacks. Into the notebook, each with its definition in French and two examples, one from the textbook and one found in the books you have read at C1: the figures of analogy (*comparaison*, *métaphore*, *métaphore filée*, *personnification*, *allégorie*), of substitution (*métonymie*, *synecdoque*, *périphrase*, *antonomase*), of opposition (*antithèse*, *oxymore*, *chiasme*, *antiphrase* and *ironie*, *paradoxe*), of amplification and attenuation (*hyperbole*, *gradation*, *accumulation*, *énumération*, *litote*, *euphémisme*), of insistence and construction (*anaphore*, *parallélisme*, *répétition*, *asyndète*, *polysyndète*, *hypallage*, *zeugma*, *ellipse*, *prétérition*, *prosopopée*, *apostrophe*, *question rhétorique*), of sound (*allitération*, *assonance*, *paronomase*, *homéotéleute*). Then take the *casquette* page of *Bovary* and the opening of *Combray* and find fifteen figures between them, named and quoted. These names are the vocabulary of every *commentaire* you will write; they are also what the Canard's puns are made of.
      Done when: the figures with examples in the notebook, fifteen found.

### Week 12

- [ ] **C1-227** [Study · Deep · 90 min] Versification.
      Do: your *Français 1re* textbook's pages on *la versification* and the French Wikipedia entries « Alexandrin », « Sonnet » and « Vers libre ». Into the notebook, each with a scanned example from the poems you already know by heart: the count of syllables and the *e muet* rule (sounded before a consonant inside the line, silent before a vowel and at the line's end: *Demain, dès l'aube, à l'heure où blanchit la campagne* = 12), *diérèse* and *synérèse* (*li-on* against *lion*, *vi-o-lon*), the *alexandrin* with its *césure* at the *hémistiche* (6 + 6) and the romantic *trimètre* (4 + 4 + 4: Hugo's *Je marcherai les yeux fixés sur mes pensées*), the *décasyllabe* (4 + 6) and the *octosyllabe*, *vers pairs* and *impairs* (Verlaine's *De la musique avant toute chose, / Et pour cela préfère l'Impair*), the *rimes plates* (AABB), *croisées* (ABAB), *embrassées* (ABBA), *pauvres*, *suffisantes* and *riches*, the alternance of *rimes féminines* (ending in *e muet*) and *masculines*, *enjambement*, *rejet* and *contre-rejet*, the *sonnet* (two *quatrains* and two *tercets*; the *marotique* CCD EED and the *français* CCD EDE), the *ballade* (Villon: three *dizains* and an *envoi* with a refrain), the *ode*, the *vers libre* (Apollinaire's *Zone*, Cendrars), the *poème en prose* (Baudelaire, Ponge), the *calligramme*. Then scan, with the *e muets* and *césures* marked, the Villon, Ronsard, Hugo, Nerval and Baudelaire you have learned, and write four *alexandrins* of your own that scan. The *Exercices de style* alexandrines of Week 7 get rescanned and corrected.
      Done when: terms with examples in the notebook, five poems scanned, four lines written.

### Week 16

- [ ] **C1-303** [Study · Deep · 90 min] False friends at C1, Latinisms and classical allusions.
      Do: three lists into the notebook. (1) The false friends that still catch C1 learners, each with its real meaning and the word you wanted: *actuellement* (now; « actually » = *en fait*), *éventuellement* (possibly; « eventually » = *finalement*), *sensible* (sensitive; « sensible » = *raisonnable*), *prétendre* (to claim), *décevoir* (to disappoint), *formidable* (great), *une opportunité* (a timely occasion; « an opportunity » = *une occasion*), *versatile* (fickle), *harassé* (exhausted), *achever* (to finish), *assister à* (to attend), *attendre* (to wait), *blesser* (to wound), *candide* (naive), *confus* (embarrassed), *demander* (to ask), *disposer de* (to have at one's disposal), *engagé* (committed), *ignorer* (not to know), *une injure* (an insult), *une lecture* (a reading; « a lecture » = *une conférence*, *un cours*), *une librairie* (a bookshop), *luxurieux* (lustful), *misérable* (wretched), *une phrase* (a sentence), *une rente* (unearned income), *résumer* (to sum up), *sympathique* (nice), *user* (to wear out), *vicieux* (perverted), *un délai* (a time allowed, not a delay), *la monnaie* (change), *une déception* (a disappointment), *un préservatif* (a condom), *une recette* (a recipe or takings), *la journée* against *le jour*, *un photographe* (a photographer), *gentil* (kind), *grand* (tall), *large* (wide), *un car* (a coach), *un roman* (a novel; *une nouvelle* a short story, *un récit*), *la pièce* (a room, a play, a coin), *passer un examen* (to sit it, not to pass it), *rester* (to stay), *sortir* (to go out), *supporter* (to bear), *le hasard* (chance). (2) The Latinisms every educated French text uses: *a priori*, *a posteriori*, *a fortiori*, *grosso modo*, *sine qua non*, *de facto*, *de jure*, *ad hoc*, *in extenso*, *in fine*, *in situ*, *statu quo*, *vice versa*, *idem*, *sic*, *ibid.*, *op. cit.*, *et cetera*, *alter ego*, *ex æquo*, *nota bene*, *modus vivendi*, *ipso facto*, *mea culpa*, *curriculum vitæ*, *persona non grata*, *post-scriptum*, *quiproquo*, *vade-mecum*, *ex cathedra*, *de visu*, *manu militari*, *urbi et orbi*, *mutatis mutandis*, *primo, secundo, tertio*, *casus belli*, *dixit*, *lapsus*, *alibi*, *intuitu personæ*, *nec plus ultra*; with the Académie's rule that the 1990 list accents some (*a priori* stays unaccented; *à capella* is the rectified form). (3) The allusions: *le rocher de Sisyphe*, *le supplice de Tantale*, *le fil d'Ariane*, *le talon d'Achille*, *l'épée de Damoclès*, *le tonneau des Danaïdes*, *franchir le Rubicon*, *la boîte de Pandore*, *le cheval de Troie*, *un travail de Romain*, *le chant du cygne*, *les écuries d'Augias*, *le nœud gordien*, *une victoire à la Pyrrhus*, *un dédale*, *une odyssée*, *un mentor*, *le complexe d'Œdipe*, *tomber de Charybde en Scylla*, *le lit de Procuste*, *le jugement de Pâris*, *la pomme de discorde*; and the biblical ones: *la tour de Babel*, *un jugement de Salomon*, *le bouc émissaire*, *s'en laver les mains*, *un chemin de Damas*, *le veau d'or*, *la brebis galeuse*, *le fils prodigue*, *le baiser de Judas*, *pleurer comme une Madeleine*, *vieux comme Mathusalem*, *un déluge*, *la manne*, *une traversée du désert*, *rendre à César*, *une pierre d'achoppement*, *le bon Samaritain*. Then write 300 words of commentary on this week's news using twelve of the Latinisms and allusions correctly; editor pass.
      Done when: three lists in the notebook, 300 words corrected, 40 cards over the week.

### Week 17

- [ ] **C1-322** [Study · Deep · 90 min] Québec French: phonology, lexicon, norm.
      Do: the French Wikipedia entries « Français québécois » in full and « Joual », the BDL's pages on *québécismes*, and the Office québécois's *Le Grand Dictionnaire terminologique* for five searches. Into the notebook: the phonology (the affricates *tu* = [tsy], *dire* = [dzir]; the diphthongs of long vowels, *père*, *fête*, *neige*; the lax vowels in closed syllables, *vite*, *lune*, *route*; the open *â* kept, *pâte* against *patte*; the *-oi-* as *-oé-* in popular speech, *moé, toé*; the final consonant clusters dropped, *arbre* = *arb'*; the *r* apical among the old, uvular among the young), the grammar (the question particle *-tu*: *tu viens-tu ?*, *c'est-tu loin ?*; *là* and *pis* as fillers; *à matin, à soir*; *c'est correct*; *pantoute*; *icitte*; *asteure*; *chus* for *je suis*; *ça fait que*), the lexicon in three groups: the archaisms and dialect words France lost (*char*, *souper*, *dîner* for lunch, *déjeuner* for breakfast, *la fin de semaine*, *présentement*, *une blonde*, *un chum*, *magasiner*, *dispendieux*, *jaser*, *achaler*, *niaiser*, *une patente*, *un dépanneur*, *une tuque*, *la poudrerie*, *il mouille*), the anglicisms Québec has and France does not (*un char*, *chum*, *le fun*, *checker*, *canceller*, *une joke*, *tough*, *c'est le fun*, *anyway*), and the anglicisms France has that the Office refuses (*un courriel* for *mail*, *un pourriel* for *spam*, *le clavardage* for *chat*, *une fin de semaine* for *week-end*, *le magasinage* for *shopping*, *un stationnement* for *parking*, *un traversier* for *ferry*, *un arrêt* for *stop*), and the *sacres* (*tabarnak, câlisse, ostie, crisse, ciboire*) with their softened forms (*tabarnouche, câline*). Then the norm: the Office's position on the *français standard en usage au Québec*; the rectifications applied; *la ministre*, *l'autrice*, *la professeure* long before France. Then listen to ten minutes of *Tout le monde en parle* and transcribe one minute, marking each feature.
      Done when: notes in the notebook, one minute transcribed and annotated, 20 cards.

Into the notebook, each with a sentence from *Le Monde* or the *Diplo* found this week (search the digital edition): the support-verb collocations (*porter plainte*, *prendre acte de*, *mettre en œuvre*, *faire état de*, *faire l'objet de*, *tirer les conséquences de*, *rendre compte de*, *donner lieu à*, *prendre en compte*, *remettre en cause*, *mettre en lumière*, *mettre en évidence*, *jouer un rôle*, *susciter un débat*, *soulever une question*, *revêtir une importance*, *poser problème*, *faire défaut*, *faire face à*, *avoir recours à*, *prendre position*, *porter atteinte à*, *mettre fin à*, *mener à bien*, *tenir compte de*, *faire preuve de*, *prêter à confusion*, *donner suite à*, *faire l'unanimité*, *passer sous silence*), the prepositional phrases of argument (*à l'issue de*, *au terme de*, *dans le cadre de*, *en vertu de*, *à l'instar de*, *à l'aune de*, *au regard de*, *en dépit de*, *à défaut de*, *faute de*, *à l'encontre de*, *au détriment de*, *à l'insu de*, *en marge de*, *au sein de*, *à l'égard de*, *sous réserve de*, *à l'exception de*, *compte tenu de*, *eu égard à*), the adverbial connectors of the *registre soutenu* (*au demeurant*, *en l'occurrence*, *force est de constater que*, *il n'en demeure pas moins que*, *à telle enseigne que*, *tant s'en faut*, *qui plus est*, *de surcroît*, *en revanche*, *en outre*, *partant*, *dès lors*, *somme toute*, *à cet égard*, *à ce titre*, *en tout état de cause*, *le cas échéant*, *à juste titre*, *à tort ou à raison*, *pour autant*, *du reste*, *au reste*, *certes*, *or*), and the nominalisations the press prefers (*la mise en place*, *la prise en charge*, *la remise en cause*, *le dépôt d'une plainte*, *la hausse / la baisse de*, *l'entrée en vigueur*, *la montée en puissance*, *la levée de boucliers*, *la passe d'armes*, *le bras de fer*, *le coup de théâtre*, *le tour de vis*, *le tour de passe-passe*). Then rewrite one of your bank texts with fifteen of these; editor pass; 40 cards over the week.

## Block 3: Weeks 19–27, the seventeenth century and the Francophonie

### Week 19

- [ ] **C1-360** [Study · Deep · 90 min] *Le français classique*: reading the sixteenth and seventeenth centuries.
      Do: the French Wikipedia entries « Français classique » and « Histoire de l'orthographe française » (the sections on the sixteenth and seventeenth centuries), and BU's historical notes where the steps' points send you. On Gallica, open the 1694 first edition of the *Dictionnaire de l'Académie française* (search « Dictionnaire de l'Académie françoise 1694 ») and read the entries *honneste*, *estre* and *françois*; then open a seventeenth-century Molière (the 1682 *Œuvres de Monsieur de Molière*) at the first page of *Tartuffe* and read it beside your modern edition. Into the notebook: the spelling (the *s* before a consonant that became the circumflex: *estre, teste, mesme, hostel*; *-oi-* where modern French has *-ai-* in *françois, avoit, connoître*, not reformed until 1835; *y* for *i* in *luy, moy*; *-ez* for *-és*; *&* for *et*; *long-temps*, *très-bien*; the *u/v* and *i/j* not yet distinct in 1600, settled by 1694); the grammar (*ne ... point* as the ordinary negation; the object pronoun before the modal, *je le veux voir*; *icelui*; the *passé simple* in dialogue; *on* for *nous* already; *dont* used freely; the *subjonctif imparfait* in every mouth); and the lexicon, the thirty words whose meaning moved, each with the old and new sense: *étonner* (to thunderstrike), *ennui* (grief), *gêne* (torture), *succès* (outcome, good or bad), *honnête homme* (the cultivated gentleman), *fortune* (fate), *amant* (suitor), *maîtresse* (the woman one courts), *tout à l'heure* (at once), *galant*, *esprit* (wit), *charme* (spell), *ravir* (to carry off), *flamme* (love), *feu* (passion), *fer* (sword), *hymen* (marriage), *sang* (lineage), *foi* (word given), *générosité* (nobility of soul), *vertu* (strength of character), *objet* (the beloved), *soin* (care, attentions), *transport* (fit of passion), *courage* (heart), *funeste* (deadly), *déplaisir* (grief), *cruel* (unresponsive), *jaloux* (zealous), *doute* (fear). Then read one page of *Des cannibales* in the modernised edition you will use this week and, on Wikisource, the same page in the 1595 spelling; list ten differences.
      Done when: Gallica entries read, the three lists in the notebook, the page compared.

      Do: the Belgian weeks begin. On rtbf.be/auvio (most of it open abroad) watch the newest *JT 19h30* in full (30 minutes; the Brussels accent of the presenters, the federal, regional and community politics: *le fédéral*, *la Région wallonne*, *la Fédération Wallonie-Bruxelles*, *la Chambre*, *le Premier ministre*, *le MR*, *le PS*, *Ecolo*, *la N-VA* on the Flemish side) and twenty minutes of *Le Grand Cactus* (the RTBF comedy panel; on Auvio and YouTube). Before them, the French Wikipedia entry « Français de Belgique » (phonology: the long vowels, *brun* and *brin* kept distinct, the *w* of *wagon* as [w]; lexicon: *septante, nonante, déjeuner / dîner / souper, une drache, un kot, un chicon, un GSM, une aubette, une farde, un essuie, une dringuelle, une jatte, un pistolet* the bread roll, *ça va ?* as hello, *à tantôt*). Into the notebook, each with its register label and a sentence: one hundred idioms of everyday French (*avoir le cafard*, *mettre les pieds dans le plat*, *tomber dans les pommes*, *poser un lapin*, les bœufs*, *avoir un poil dans la main*, *casser les pieds*, *chercher midi à quatorze heures*, *donner sa langue au chat*, *avoir le bras long*, *être à côté de la plaque*, *péter les plombs*, *avoir la tête dans le guidon*, *se serrer la ceinture*, *mettre du beurre dans les épinards*, *rouler quelqu'un dans la farine*, *avoir du pain sur la planche*, *ne pas y aller par quatre chemins*, *se faire des cheveux blancs*, *tomber des nues*, *avoir le cœur sur la main*, *être au bout du rouleau*, *en avoir ras le bol*, *ça ne casse pas trois pattes à un canard*, *il n'y a pas de quoi fouetter un chat*, *revenons à nos moutons*, *c'est une autre paire de manches*, *mettre la clé sous la porte*, *prendre la mouche*, *avoir une dent contre*, *se tenir à carreau*, *filer à l'anglaise*, *avoir un coup de foudre*, *se noyer dans un verre d'eau*, *faire d'une pierre deux coups*, *coûter les yeux de la tête*, *être dans la lune*, *avoir les yeux plus gros que le ventre*, social*, *la casse sociale*, *le quoi qu'il en coûte*, *le en même temps*, *la Macronie*, *le vieux monde*, *le dégagisme*, *la France d'en bas*, *la France périphérique*, *les bobos*, *le pays réel*, *le ras-le-bol fiscal*). ### Week 23

- [ ] **C1-441** [Study · Deep · 90 min] Belgian and Swiss French: the varieties written out.
      Do: the French Wikipedia entries « Français de Belgique » and « Français de Suisse » in full, and the BDL's pages on *belgicismes* and *helvétismes*; Michel Francard's *Dictionnaire des belgicismes* (De Boeck) if you can find it, otherwise the Wikipedia lists. Into the notebook, in three columns (Belgium, Switzerland, France): the phonology (Belgium: the long vowels kept and the final vowel lengthening, *ami* / *amie*; the four nasals with *brun* distinct from *brin*; *w* as [w] in *wagon*; the *r* often apical in Liège; *-tion* with the *t* as [s] but *ui* as [wi] in *huit*; the Brussels and Liège intonations. Switzerland: the slow rhythm, the lengthened penultimate, *septante, huitante, nonante*, the same four nasals, the open *-ée* against *-é*), the lexicon (Belgium: *la drache*, *le kot*, *le chicon*, *le GSM*, *l'aubette*, *la farde*, *l'essuie*, *le pistolet*, *la jatte*, *la dringuelle*, *la mitraillette*, *le bourgmestre*, *l'échevin*, *le fédéral*, *la Communauté*, *le bachelier* for the degree, *le blocus* for revision, *la rhéto*, *le mémoire*; Switzerland: *le natel*, *la panosse*, *le cornet*, *le linge*, *la poutze*, *la votation*, *l'initiative*, *le Conseil fédéral*, *la commune*, *le cycle d'orientation*, *la maturité*, *un bancomat*, *le car postal*, *la raclette*, *le carnotzet*; both: *déjeuner / dîner / souper*, *tantôt*, *une fois*, *savoir* for *pouvoir* in Belgium, *s'il vous plaît* handing over, *ça joue*, *service*, *santé* for *à vos souhaits*), the institutions (Belgium: the federal State, three Regions, three Communities, the *N-VA*, the linguistic *facilités*, the *Fourons*; Switzerland: the Confederation, 26 cantons, four national languages, *la Suisse romande* against *la Suisse alémanique* and the *Röstigraben*, direct democracy). Then listen to ten minutes of the RTBF *JT* and ten of the RTS *19h30* and transcribe one minute of each, marking each feature.
      Done when: the three columns in the notebook, two minutes transcribed and annotated, 20 cards.

### Week 24

- [ ] **C1-461** [Study · Deep · 90 min] African and Maghrebi French.
      Do: the French Wikipedia entries « Français d'Afrique », « Nouchi », « Français du Maghreb » and « Organisation internationale de la Francophonie » (the figures: more than 300 million speakers, most of the growth in Africa, Kinshasa the largest French-speaking city after Paris), and the *Dictionnaire des mots nouveaux du français d'Afrique* pages you can find online (the *Inventaire des particularités lexicales du français en Afrique noire* is the reference). Into the notebook: West and Central Africa (the phonology: the *r* apical or rolled, the syllable-timed rhythm, the *e* not dropped, the nasals opened; the lexicon of Abidjan, Dakar, Yaoundé, Kinshasa: *le maquis* for a bar-restaurant, *la gare routière*, *ambiancer*, *enceinter*, *cadeauter*, *un sapeur* and *la Sape*, *le pagne*, *un boubou*, *le marabout*, *une deuxième bureau* for a mistress, *le gombo* for a bribe or a side job, *un bana-bana*, *le wax*, *la go* for a girl, *gbagba*, *c'est comment ?* as hello, *on est ensemble*, *il y a pas de problème*; the *nouchi* of Abidjan: *enjailler*, *un gaou*, *s'en foutre*, *le djandjou*; the grammar: *là* as a demonstrative, *même* as an intensifier, the *passé composé* for the remote past, *il faut* + infinitive as a polite order, *faire* with a new object range, the French of the administration kept from colonial times: *un cadre*, *le chef de service*, *une note de service*, *le fonctionnaire*), the Maghreb (the code-switching with *darija*, *inchallah*, *wallah*, *khoya*, *la hogra*, *le makhzen*, *le bled*, *le FLN*, *les harkis*, *le hirak*, *la francophonie refusée* in Algeria and the 2022 turn to English in Algerian schools; the Algerian press in French, *El Watan*, *Liberté*), and the politics of the OIF (the sommets, the *Jeux de la Francophonie*, Senghor, Bourguiba, Diori and Sihanouk as founders, the *TV5Monde* and *RFI* networks). Then listen to ten minutes of RFI's *Journal Afrique* and ten of TV5Monde's *Journal Afrique* and transcribe one minute of each, marking features.
      Done when: lists in the notebook, two minutes transcribed and annotated, 20 cards.

### Week 25

- [ ] **C1-481** [Study · Deep · 90 min] Antillean French and créole basics.
      Do: the French Wikipedia entries « Créole antillais », « Créole martiniquais » (grammar section), « Créolité » and « Français des Antilles »; Chamoiseau, Bernabé and Confiant's *Éloge de la créolité* (1989, Gallimard; the first ten pages, which are the manifesto: « Ni Européens, ni Africains, ni Asiatiques, nous nous proclamons Créoles »); the first chapter of Chamoiseau's *Texaco* (Goncourt 1992; you will read it whole at C2) for its French. Into the notebook: the créole basics for recognition (the lexicon is French; the grammar is not: *mwen, ou, i, nou, zot, yo* as pronouns; no gender or number agreement; the verb invariable with the aspect markers before it, *ka* for the progressive (*mwen ka manjé* = I am eating), *té* for the past, *ké* for the future, *té ka*, *té ké*; the definite article after the noun, *chimen-an* the road; *sa ou fè ?* how are you, *mwen la* I'm fine, *bonjou*, *mèsi*, *an lòt soley* goodbye, *doudou*, *ti*, *bèlè*, *zouk*, *le béké*, *le bagay*; the *an tjè* for *en cœur*), the Antillean French (the *r* weakened, the *é/è* merger, *un ti punch*, *le colombo*, *la yole*, *le carnaval*, *vidé*, *la chabine*, *le bokit*, *les Antilles*, *la Guadeloupe*, *la Martinique*, *la Guyane*, *la Réunion* as *départements d'outre-mer*, *le DROM*, *l'Hexagone* against *la métropole*, *le CHU*, *la vie chère*, the 2009 strike), the three concepts (Césaire's *négritude*, Glissant's *antillanité* and *créolisation*, Chamoiseau's *créolité*). Then listen to ten minutes of Martinique La 1ère's *journal* (la1ere.francetvinfo.fr/martinique; the *JT* in French with créole inside it) and to a Kassav' song (*Zouk la sé sel médikaman nou ni*) with its lyrics, and note what you follow.
      Done when: lists in the notebook, ten minutes heard and noted, 20 cards.
