### Week 26

- [ ] **C1-502** [Study · Deep · 90 min] The prosody of radio speech and of the stage.
      Do: the French Wikipedia entries « Accent tonique en français », « Liaison en français » (the table of *liaisons obligatoires, facultatives, interdites*) and « E caduc »; BU's chapter on the *liaison*. Into the notebook: the group-final stress and the absence of word stress (*un pro-fes-SEUR de fran-ÇAIS*), the *accent d'insistance* on the first consonant-initial syllable for emphasis (*c'est FOR-midable*, *une CA-tastrophe*), the continuation rise at the end of each group and the fall at the end of the sentence, the question rise; the liaisons: obligatory (determiner + noun *les_amis*, pronoun + verb *ils_ont*, *très_utile*, *en_avant*, *tout_à fait*, after monosyllabic prepositions *dans_un*, in fixed phrases *les_États-Unis*, *de temps_en temps*), optional and marking register (*je suis_allé*, *pas_encore*, *des enfants_intelligents*; the more liaisons, the more formal: newsreaders make almost all of them, *Quotidien* makes almost none), forbidden (after *et*, before *h aspiré*, *les haricots*, after a singular noun, *un enfant/aimable*, before *oui*, *onze*, in *comment allez-vous* against *comment/est-il*), and the *cuirs* and *velours* (the false liaisons of insecure speech, *il va-t-aller*, *moi-z-aussi*); the *e caduc*: dropped in fast speech by the *loi des trois consonnes* (*je ne sais pas* → *chais pas*; *samedi* with it kept, *médecin* with it dropped), kept in careful speech and in the South, always pronounced in verse before a consonant; the stage diction of the Comédie-Française (every *e muet* before a consonant, the *liaisons* all made, the *r* sometimes rolled in older recordings, the alexandrine's two accents at the *césure* and the end). Then the audit: record one minute of this morning's France Inter *journal* shadowed (the daily) and one minute of *Phèdre* read, and ask your AI voice mode, with the recordings played to it, to list every liaison missed or wrongly made and every *e caduc* kept or dropped against the norm of each register.
      Done when: notes in the notebook, two recordings audited, the list of corrections drilled ten times.

### Week 27: Milestone C1.3

- [ ] **C1-521** [Study · Deep · 90 min] The academic register: the French article, hedging and citation.
      Do: on cairn.info or openedition.org (free for most articles older than a few years; persee.fr for the archive), read one French-language journal article of about 6000 words on a subject of this level (search « Proust » on *Revue d'histoire littéraire de la France*, or « laïcité » on *Archives de sciences sociales des religions*, or anything the QSJ bibliographies pointed to). Into the notebook: the conventions (*introduction* with *état de la question* and *problématique*, *parties* numbered I, II, III with sub-parts 1, 2, the *conclusion* with *perspectives*; the French tolerance for the long sentence and the abstract noun; the *nous de modestie* and the impersonal *on*; *il convient de*, *il importe de*, *nous nous proposons de*, *dans un premier temps*, *force est de constater*, *il apparaît que*, *on peut faire l'hypothèse que*, *sans doute* meaning probably, *il n'est pas certain que* + subjunctive, *à cet égard*, *en ce sens*, *à bien des égards*, *pour notre part*, *au demeurant*, *en définitive*, *ce faisant*, *partant*), the citation (*(Compagnon, 2006, p. 45)* in the human sciences, the footnote with *op. cit.*, *ibid.*, *art. cit.*, *cf.*, *sq.*, *passim*, *dir.*, *éd.*, *t.* for *tome*, *vol.*, *coll.*, the French title conventions of C1-169), and thirty academic collocations harvested from the article (*il convient de souligner*, *il ressort de*, *à la lumière de*, *en regard de*, *faire l'objet d'une étude*, *s'inscrire dans*, *rendre compte de*, *mettre au jour*, *mettre en regard*, *faire état de*, *au prisme de*, *dans le sillage de*, *à rebours de*, *en creux*, *à l'aune de*). Then rewrite one B2 opinion paragraph in the academic register; editor pass.
      Done when: article read and analysed, lists in the notebook, paragraph rewritten and corrected, 30 cards.

      3. **Listening**: a *La Trêve* episode rewatched with the sound only (any one): 12 facts, 10 right; an RTBF *JT* and an RTS *19h30* of the day: the five headlines of each, no factual error; a *Journal Afrique* of 25 minutes: 8 of 10 points; a *Les Grosses Têtes* segment of 15 minutes: eight jokes explained; a *Les Chemins de la philosophie* episode: a 10-sentence summary with the argument's structure.
      4. **Reading**: a page of the 1694 *Dictionnaire* on Gallica read aloud and paraphrased in modern French, no misreading; a cairn.info article's introduction (1500 words): the *problématique* and the plan in 5 sentences; a Québec, a Belgian and a Swiss newspaper article (*Le Devoir*, *Le Soir*, *Le Temps*): three regionalisms found in each.
      5. **Speaking**: presentation 3 at most 3 errors; examiner list at most 4; the seminar transcripts show register control (the assistant confirms no *familier* forms in the academic parts and no *soutenu* ones in the register day's *familier* version).
      6. **Writing**: Appendix I task 20 (an *essai* of 1200 words, « La littérature française est-elle encore française ? », citing at least four works of this level with precision, including two Francophone ones), 3 hours over two days: at most 1 error per 100 words, the academic conventions of C1-521, a clear thesis and conclusion; dictée rate of the last two at or under 3 percent.
      7. **Spelling**: Projet Voltaire's level test at *Excellence* begun with a score of 600 or more on its scale.
      8. **Habits**: 27 weeks complete; QSJ 9; films 14; poems 27; the Canon table has Montaigne, Pascal, *Clèves*, *Phèdre*, *Tartuffe*, *Le Misanthrope*, *Germinal*, Césaire, Daoud, Mabanckou, *Hadrien* with their texts; the Varieties table has Belgian, Swiss, African, Maghrebi and Antillean rows; the commute slot and free reading logged on at least 130 of the last 162 study days each.
      Fail rules: 1 → Anki fortnight; 2 → C1-360, C1-420, C1-441, C1-461, C1-502, C1-521 reread and the exercises redone; 3 → LRS on four *JT* segments of each country and four *Grosses Têtes* segments; 4 → six Gallica pages and six regional articles intensively; 5 → daily seminars two weeks; 6 → the *essai* rewritten with the editor, then a second one; 7 → four Projet Voltaire sessions.

## Block 4: Weeks 28–33, the eighteenth century, the comedians and the DALF

### Week 28

- [ ] **C1-541** [Study · Deep · 90 min] The bac philosophy *dissertation*.
      Do: your *Terminale* philosophy manual's method pages (every publisher has twenty pages on the *dissertation* and the *explication de texte*) and the programme on eduscol (the seventeen *notions*: *l'art, le bonheur, la conscience, le devoir, l'État, l'inconscient, la justice, le langage, la liberté, la nature, la raison, la religion, la science, la technique, le temps, le travail, la vérité*; the *repères* such as *absolu/relatif, en fait/en droit, objectif/subjectif, persuader/convaincre*). Into the notebook: the subject is always a question (« Peut-on être libre sans être responsable ? », « La vérité dépend-elle de nous ? », « Le travail nous rend-il plus humains ? »); the *analyse des termes* (each defined, the *présupposé* found, the *paradoxe*), the *problématique* (the subject reformulated as a tension in one sentence, « Si ... alors ... ; or ... ; dès lors, ... ? »), the plan in three parts that *progress* (a first answer from common sense, its limits, a reformulation), each part with two arguments and one *référence* (an author read in the manual: Platon, Aristote, Descartes, Pascal (you have him), Rousseau (you will have him next week), Kant, Hegel, Nietzsche, Freud, Sartre (you have him), Arendt, Bergson, Merleau-Ponty), the *exemple* précis, the *introduction* and *conclusion* in the form the *correcteurs* expect, 4 hours at the bac; the marking (the official *attendus*: a *problématique*, a *progression*, *références* used and not dropped, *rigueur conceptuelle*). Read one *corrigé* from the *annales* (the manual's or annabac.com's) and mark its *problématique*, its three parts and its references. Then, in 40 minutes, write the complete plan and introduction for « Faut-il préférer le bonheur à la vérité ? ».
      Done when: method in the notebook, *corrigé* marked, plan and introduction written and corrected.

### Week 29

- [ ] **C1-560** [Study · Deep · 90 min] The DALF C1 and the *synthèse de documents*.
      Do: download the DALF C1 *exemple de sujet* from france-education-international.fr (the *candidat* and *examinateur* booklets, the audio, the *corrigés* and the *grilles d'évaluation*). Into the notebook: the four *épreuves*: *compréhension de l'oral* (40 minutes; a long recording heard twice with questions, then short ones heard once), *compréhension des écrits* (50 minutes; a literary or journalistic text and a long argumentative one), *production écrite* (2 h 30; a *synthèse* of two or three documents in 220–240 words and an *essai argumenté* of about 250 words on the same theme, in the *lettre formelle*, *article* or *contribution à un débat* form the subject names), *production orale* (30 minutes after 60 of preparation: an *exposé* of 10 minutes from two or three documents, then a 20-minute *entretien*); 25 points each, 50 of 100 to pass with at least 5 in each; the *domaines* (*lettres et sciences humaines* or *sciences*, chosen at registration). The *synthèse* method, which no English exam has: neutral, in the third person, no opinion of your own, no quotation longer than three words, every idea attributed (*selon le premier document*, *l'auteur du second texte souligne que*), the documents confronted not summarised in turn, an *introduction* naming the documents and the theme, two or three *parties* by idea, a one-sentence *conclusion*, the word count respected to within ten percent. Then do the *exemple*'s *synthèse* under its time (about 75 minutes of the 2 h 30), count the words, compare with the *corrigé*'s model, editor pass against the *grille* (*respect de la consigne*, *traitement des documents*, *cohérence*, *lexique*, *morphosyntaxe*).
      Done when: notes in the notebook, one *synthèse* written, marked and filed.

### Week 30

- [ ] **C1-582** [Study · Deep · 90 min] *Le Bon Usage*: the audit of the difficult points.
      Do: forty points, each looked up in BU (its index is the way in) and the Académie's *Dire, ne pas dire*, and written into the notebook as the rule and an example: *après que* + indicative (*après qu'il est parti*), *avant que* + subjunctive; *malgré que* avoided; *pallier quelque chose* (no *à*); *se rappeler quelque chose* against *se souvenir de*; *de manière à ce que* accepted, *de façon que*; *au niveau de* only for levels; *par contre* accepted (Voltaire used it) against *en revanche*; *suite à* avoided in writing (*à la suite de*); *la plupart ont*; *cela* in writing, *ça* in speech; *ce qui* / *ce que* (*ce dont je parle*, *ce à quoi je pense*); *second* when there are two, *deuxième* when more (a tendency, not a rule); *davantage* against *plus* (*davantage de*); *an* against *année*, *jour* / *journée*, *matin* / *matinée*; *amener* (people) against *apporter* (things), *emmener* / *emporter*; *décade* (ten days) against *décennie*; *soi-disant* (only of persons who say so themselves) against *prétendu*; *à l'attention de* (on a letter) against *à l'intention de* (for the benefit of); *quoique* / *quoi que*, *quelque* / *quel que* (from Week 5); *tout* (from Week 5); *demi*, *nu*, *ci-joint*; *vu, attendu, excepté*; *leur* invariable as a pronoun; *aucuns frais* (plural with a plural-only noun); *des plus* + plural adjective; *l'un et l'autre sont*, *l'un ou l'autre est*; *ni ... ni* with the verb negated; *sans que* without *ne*; *ne ... que* and *seulement* not doubled; *la plupart du temps*; *pécuniaire* not *pécunier*; *dilemme* not *dilemne*; *infarctus*; *rémunérer* not *rénumérer*; *en l'occurrence*; *vis-à-vis de*; *faire montre de*; *à l'envi*; *au temps pour moi* (the Académie's spelling) against *autant pour moi*; *tant qu'à faire*; *voire* without *même*; *de par*; *par ailleurs*; *d'ores et déjà*; *en outre*; *hormis*; *sitôt* / *si tôt*; *plutôt* / *plus tôt*; *quelquefois* / *quelques fois*; *parce que* / *par ce que*; *près de* / *prêt à*; *censé* / *sensé*; *conjecture* / *conjoncture*; *acception* / *acceptation*; *éminent* / *imminent*; *notable* / *notoire*; *affleurer* / *effleurer*; *percepteur* / *précepteur*; *une espèce de* (always feminine); *un après-midi* or *une*; *un pétale*, *une oasis*, *un tentacule*, *une orbite*, *un haltère*, *une échappatoire*, *un antidote*, *un astérisque*, *un effluve*. Then search your corrected portfolio for every point that appears in its error tables and make 30 cards.
      Done when: forty points in the notebook, 30 cards.

### Week 31

- [ ] **C1-601** [Study · Deep · 90 min] The history of French in ten dates, on Gallica.
      Do: the French Wikipedia entries « Histoire de la langue française » (the sections from the sixteenth century on) and « Réforme de l'orthographe française de 1835 ». Into the notebook, the ten dates with one sentence each: 842 (the *Serments de Strasbourg*, the first French text), 1539 (the *ordonnance de Villers-Cotterêts*: French in the courts), 1549 (du Bellay's *Défense*), 1635 (the Académie), 1694 (its first dictionary), 1794 (the abbé Grégoire's report on the *patois* and the Revolution's French-only policy), 1835 (the sixth edition of the dictionary: *-ais* for *-ois*, *enfants* for *enfans*), 1878 and 1935 (the lesser reforms; the hyphen in *très-bien* dropped), 1882 (Jules Ferry's school), 1990 (the rectifications), 1992 (article 2 of the Constitution: « La langue de la République est le français »), 1994 (the loi Toubon). Then on Gallica: Hugo's manuscript of *Les Misérables* (search « Les Misérables manuscrit » in Gallica's *manuscrits*; the BnF holds it and has digitised it) for ten minutes, to see the hand and the corrections; the 1857 *Bovary* again for a page of Part III; and an 1830s newspaper page (the *Journal des débats* or *La Presse* in Gallica's *presse*) for the pre-1835 spelling. List fifteen forms that have changed since 1830 with their modern equivalents.
      Done when: ten dates, fifteen forms, in the notebook.

### Week 32

- [ ] **C1-619** [Study · Deep · 90 min] Argot, verlan and *le langage des jeunes*.
      Do: the French Wikipedia entries « Verlan », « Argot » and « Français contemporain des cités », and *Le Petit Robert*'s labels on each word you note (*arg.*, *fam.*, *pop.*, *vulg.*). Into the notebook: the *verlan* mechanism (syllable inversion, with the vowel adjusted: *femme* → *meuf*, *flic* → *keuf*, *lourd* → *relou*, *louche* → *chelou*, *fou* → *ouf*, *fête* → *teuf*, *énervé* → *vénère*, *choper* → *pécho*, *bizarre* → *zarbi*, *merci* → *cimer*, *frère* → *reuf*, *mère* → *reum*, *père* → *reup*, *arabe* → *beur* → *rebeu* (verlan of verlan), *juif* → *feuj*, *cité* → *téci*, *pourri* → *ripou*, *tomber* → *béton* (*laisse béton*), *moi* → *ouam*), the *argot classique* of Audiard and Simonin that films of the 1950s and *Le Trou* use (*le pognon*, *le flingue*, *la gonzesse*, *le mec*, *le pote*, *la bagnole*, *le boulot*, *la taule*, *le flic*, *bouffer*, *piger*, *se barrer*, *gueuler*, *la trouille*, *le pinard*, *la clope*, *le pieu*, *roupiller*, *le toubib*, *le bahut*, *le pèze*, *le fric*, *les fringues*, *la came*, *le tapin*), the *langage des jeunes* of the 2010s and 2020s with its Arabic, Romani and English loans (*wesh*, *kiffer*, *le seum*, *le bail*, *la daronne / le daron*, *le sang* for mate, *en vrai*, *de ouf*, *grave*, *trop*, *c'est chaud*, *ça passe crème*, *un bolosse*, *un gadjo*, *une gadji*, *le khey / la kheyette* no longer current, *askip* (*à ce qu'il paraît*), *bg* (*beau gosse*), *frérot*, *la hess*, *être dans le mal*, *un boloss*, *mytho*, *le bif*, *chiller*, *flex*, *une dinguerie*, *c'est la base*, *ça fait sens* as an anglicism the Académie rejects), the grammar of the register (*ne* dropped, *on* only, *y a* for *il y a*, *genre* and *style* as fillers, *trop* for *très*, the dislocations, *t'as vu*, *tu vois*, the lengthened final syllable of the *banlieue* accent, the *r* fronted), and what it means to use any of it as a foreigner (recognise everything; produce only *familier*, never *argot*, unless you are quoting). Then transcribe two minutes of a *Validé* trailer or a Kaaris or PNL song with its lyrics, or two minutes of *Les Sauvages*, and gloss every marked word.

## Block 5: C1 exit check (Weeks 34–36)

Take Parts 1–12 over at most ten days. Week 34's remaining content: poems or songs 34, 35 and 36 (Appendix J: Michaux, Brassens *Les Passantes*, Baudelaire *Correspondances*), one a week, done alongside, with the poem or song pattern.

- [ ] **C1-665** [Produce · Focused · 30 min] Then and now, part 1: listen to `C1-012`, the B2 baseline recording and presentation 3; write 5 improvements and 3 remaining problems.
      Do: listen, write in French.
      Done when: the eight lines are written.
- [ ] **C1-666** [Check] **Part 1, Vocabulary.**
      Pass if: Anki at least 12,500 cards, 9200 mature, retention at least 85 percent; FREQ spot check of ranks 1–10,000: at least 90 of 100 random words known, with their gender for nouns.
- [ ] **C1-667** [Check] **Part 2, Language.**
      Do: Appendix D: 30 translations and 10 rewrites, 75 minutes, no dictionary. Marking: any error of grammar, agreement, idiom, register, spelling, accent or typography counts; a rewrite passes only if it meets the stated instruction and is error-free.
      Pass if: at least 34 of 40.
- [ ] **C1-668** [Check] **Part 3, Reading.**
      Do: (a) Appendix E, 10 questions, 25 minutes. (b) The DALF C1 *compréhension des écrits* paper of the *exemple de sujet* again under its 50 minutes. (c) A page of the 1694 *Dictionnaire de l'Académie* and a page of the 1857 *Bovary* on Gallica, read aloud and paraphrased in modern French; a Montaigne page in the 1595 spelling on Wikisource, paraphrased.
      Pass if: (a) at least 9 of 10; (b) at least 20 of 25; (c) no misreading, and the register of each identified with a justification.
- [ ] **C1-669** [Check] **Part 4, Listening.**
      Do: (a) *Le Bureau des légendes*, the last episode of season 5, no subtitles: 15 facts, checked on a second viewing with subtitles. (b) The DALF C1 *compréhension de l'oral* paper again under its 40 minutes. (c) A lecture in French of 45 minutes (a Collège de France lecture, a *Les Chemins de la philosophie* episode or a university lecture on YouTube): a 12-sentence structured summary. (d) A *Les Grosses Têtes* segment of 15 minutes: each speaker's position and the joke that got the biggest laugh, explained; a *Quotidien* opening of 10 minutes: five jokes explained. (e) Radio-Canada's *Tout le monde en parle*, the RTBF *JT*, the RTS *19h30* and TV5Monde's *Journal Afrique*, 10 minutes each: the five headlines of each. (f) A dictée of 3 minutes from a Pivot final you have not done.
      Pass if: (a) 13 of 15; (b) at least 20 of 25; (c) structure and conclusion right, no factual error; (d) all positions and all jokes; (e) 18 of 20 headlines; (f) at most 3 percent of words wrong.
- [ ] **C1-670** [Check] **Part 5, Writing: the EAF commentaire and the bac philosophy dissertation.**
      Do: two sittings on two days, closed book except the text and a dictionary. (1) An EAF *commentaire composé* on a *sujet* from the past papers you have not used (eduscol or an académie site; take the *commentaire* of the *série générale*), 4 hours as at the bac, marked by yourself and then by the editor instruction against the official *grille*, out of 20. (2) A bac philosophy *dissertation* on a *sujet* from the last session's *annales* you have not used, 4 hours as at the bac, marked against the published *corrigé* and by the editor as a *correcteur*, out of 20.
      Pass if: both at 12 of 20 or more by the editor's mark; at most 1 error per 100 words in each; the *commentaire* has its *problématique*, two or three *axes* and at least fifteen *procédés* analysed; the *dissertation* has its *problématique*, a progression in three parts and at least four *références* used.
- [ ] **C1-671** [Check] **Part 6, Writing: the DALF synthèse and the essai.**
      Do: the DALF C1 *production écrite* paper (a *sujet* not used in Weeks 29 and 32 if one exists, otherwise the first again), 2 h 30: the *synthèse* (220–240 words) and the *essai argumenté*; then a 1200-word *essai* in two days, « Qu'est-ce qu'une langue mondiale, et le français en est-il une ? », citing at least four works or programmes of this level including two from outside France. Editor pass on all three.
      Pass if: the *synthèse* within the word count, neutral, every idea attributed, marked at least 15 of 25 on the *grille*; the *essai argumenté* at least 15 of 25; the long *essai* at most 1 error per 100 words with a thesis, a structured argument, the academic conventions of C1-521 and a conclusion.
- [ ] **C1-672** [Check] **Part 7, Speaking.**
      Do: Recording protocol. (1) The DALF C1 *production orale* in exam form on a *sujet* not used before: 60 minutes' preparation, the 10-minute exposé, the 20-minute *entretien* with the AI examiner, marked on the *grille*. (2) A 25-minute seminar conversation on two of the books of this level, one of them Francophone. (3) A 5-minute unprepared reflection: « Que signifie maîtriser une langue à ce niveau, et que me manque-t-il encore ? » (4) A 3-minute reading of a *Combray* paragraph, compared with Dussollier, and twelve alexandrines of *Phèdre*, compared with the Comédie-Française recording. (5) The register day: one story told three times, *soutenu*, *courant*, *familier*, and the assistant asked to list every form out of register.
      Pass if: (1) at least 15 of 25 on the *grille*, at most 3 errors in the exposé, no silence over 2 seconds; (2) at least 45 utterances of 15+ words, error list at most 5, register maintained, every challenge answered with a reference; (3) at most 4 errors; (4) rhythm, liaison and vowel length matching the professional reading in all but three places in each; (5) at most 2 forms out of register in each version.
- [ ] **C1-673** [Check] **Part 8, Pronunciation and varieties.**
      Do: record the 20 sentences of Appendix G in three registers as instructed. Play a 5-minute conversation recording to your AI voice mode and ask it to note any word it would not have understood, any liaison missed or wrongly made, and any place where the intonation was not native. Then the varieties: play yourself, blind (a friend's choice or a shuffled playlist), six one-minute clips from Appendix G part 2's list (Paris, Marseille, Québec, Brussels, Geneva, Dakar or Abidjan, Algiers, Fort-de-France) and name each.
      Pass if: the assistant notes at most 2 words, at most 3 liaison faults and at most 3 intonation places; 5 of 6 varieties right.
- [ ] **C1-674** [Check] **Part 9, Official samples: the DALF C1 and the Certificat Voltaire.**
      Do: (a) The DALF C1 *exemple de sujet* in full (the four papers, taken over two days under their times, the oral in exam form with the AI examiner), marked against the keys and the *grilles*; if you can, register for the real DALF C1 at the nearest centre (sessions several times a year; about 200 euros) and sit it. (b) The Certificat Voltaire: sit it at a centre (about 60 euros, 3 hours, sessions monthly in most cities) or do the full practice test on projet-voltaire.fr under its time; the score on its 1000-point scale.
      Pass if: (a) at least 60 of 100 in all with at least 12 of 25 in each paper (the real exam passes at 50 with 5 in each; you train higher); (b) at least 700 (the *niveau affaires*), or the certificate in hand at that level.
- [ ] **C1-675** [Check] **Part 10, CEFR C1 self-assessment.** One concrete example per skill from the last month, in French. Pass if all five.
- [ ] **C1-676** [Check] **Part 11, Then and now.** Listen to the B1 and B2 baseline recordings, `C1-012` and this week's exposé. Five improvements and three remaining problems, in French. Pass if written.
- [ ] **C1-677** [Check] **Part 12, Habits and canon.**
      Pass if: Anki every day of the last 250 (up to 6 misses); *journal de lecture* 160 of 165 study days; the shadowing on 150 of 165; 15 exposés and 15 debates; 3 presentations; 17 dictées with the last three at or under 3 percent and 16 Projet Voltaire sessions; 16 bank texts, 14 EAF texts, the portfolio; 36 poems and songs by heart (recite ten chosen at random); 12 QSJ; 30+ issues of the Canard and 8 of the *Diplo*; the Canon table shows read in French with a text written: *Du côté de chez Swann*, *La Chute*, *Exercices de style*, *Les Mots*, *Les Années*, *Madame Bovary*, *Le Père Goriot*, *Sylvie*, *Le Rouge et le Noir*, *Les Belles-sœurs*, the three Montaigne *Essais*, the forty *Pensées*, *La Princesse de Clèves*, *Cahier d'un retour au pays natal*, *Meursault, contre-enquête*, *Phèdre*, *Tartuffe*, *Le Misanthrope*, *Germinal*, *Verre cassé*, *Mémoires d'Hadrien*, *Le Mariage de Figaro*, *Les Liaisons dangereuses*, *Au revoir là-haut*, the *Confessions* I–II, *Trois femmes puissantes*, *Les Choses*; the six *Lagarde et Michard* volumes' chapters named in the steps; at least seven free-reading novels finished; the commute slot and free reading logged on at least 200 of the last 240 study days each; the Varieties table has rows for Québec, Belgium, Switzerland, West Africa, the Maghreb and the Antilles; watched: *Le Bureau des légendes* S2–S4 and S5E1–3, *En thérapie* S1, *Hippocrate* S1, *La Trêve*, *Les Sauvages*, *Engrenages* S2, *Baron noir* S1, 27 *Quotidien*, the comedy archive set, sixteen films without subtitles and the four with their books.
- [ ] **C1-678** [Check] **Failure handling.**
      Wait at least 7 days, repair, retake only the failed parts:
      - Part 1: three weeks at 25 cards a day from FREQ and the *Diplo*; the vocabulary domain sweep again.

      - Part 6: three more *synthèses* on *Le Monde* articles; the long *essai* rewritten.
      - Part 7: daily seminars for three weeks; two more DALF-form orals; ten readings aloud compared with the recordings.
      - Part 8: shadow three audiobook chapters and two *journal* bulletins ten times each; one Francophone *JT* a day for two weeks.
      - Parts 9–12: complete what is missing; a second DALF *sujet* from the books named in C1-635.
